<meta>This page continues the discussion on the distinction between the "personality" and the "individuality" by explaining the Theosophical view of spirit and matter.</meta>

->### THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. ###<-

...between the two. Yet it is precisely that difference which provides the key to understanding Eastern philosophy, and which lies at the root of the disagreement between Theosophical and Spiritualist teachings. And though it may provoke even more hostility toward us from some Spiritualists, I must state here that Theosophy is the *true* and pure Spiritualism, while the modern movement of that name—as it is now practiced by the masses—is simply "transcendental materialism." <note>original: "transcendental materialism." The author argues that Spiritualists treat spirits as if they were just a more subtle form of physical matter rather than something truly divine and formless.</note>

ENQ. Please explain your idea more clearly.

THEO. What I mean is that although our teachings insist that spirit and matter are essentially the same, and though we say that spirit is "potential matter" and matter is simply "crystallized spirit" (for example, just as ice is solidified steam), the original and eternal condition of *everything* is not spirit, but what we might call *meta-spirit* <note>original: "meta-spirit" (from the Greek 'meta' meaning beyond). This refers to the ultimate source that exists before the division into spirit and matter.</note>. Since visible and solid matter is simply its periodic manifestation, we maintain that the term "spirit" should only be applied to the *true* individuality.

ENQ. But what is the distinction between this "true individuality" and the "I" or "Ego" <note>original: "Ego." From the Latin for "I." In Theosophy, it often refers to the permanent, reincarnating soul rather than the temporary personality.</note> that we are all conscious of?

THEO. Before I can answer you, we must agree on what you mean by "I" or "Ego." We distinguish between the simple fact of self-consciousness—the basic feeling that "I am I"—and the complex thought that "I am Mr. Smith" or "Mrs. Brown." Because we believe in a series of births for the same Ego, or reincarnation, this distinction is the fundamental pivot of the whole idea. You see, "Mr. Smith" really means a long series of daily experiences strung together by the thread of...

<summary>The author explains that Theosophy views spirit and matter as two states of one reality, distinguishing the eternal "Individuality" from the temporary "Personality" (the Ego of a single lifetime). This distinction is presented as the essential key to understanding reincarnation and Eastern philosophy.</summary>

<keywords>Theosophy, Spiritualism, Eastern philosophy, Spirit vs. Matter, Meta-spirit, Individuality, Ego, Reincarnation, Self-consciousness</keywords>

--- Page 52 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of the distinction between the "personality" (the temporary self) and the "individuality" (the eternal self) using a theatrical metaphor.</meta>

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. #

...memory, and forming what Mr. Smith calls "himself." But none of these "experiences" are really the "I" or the Ego, nor do they give "Mr. Smith" the feeling that he is himself, for he forgets the greater part of his daily experiences, and they produce the feeling of <term>Egoity</term> <note>The state of having a sense of self or "I-am-ness."</note> in him only while they last. We <term>Theosophists</term> <note>Followers of Theosophy, a philosophical movement that seeks to understand the underlying nature of the universe and the divinity of the human soul.</note>, therefore, distinguish between this bundle of "experiences," which we call the false (because it is so finite and fleeting) *personality*, and that element in man to which the feeling of "I am I" is due. It is this "I am I" which we call the *true individuality*; and we say that this "Ego" or individuality plays many parts on the stage of life, like an actor.* Let us call every new life on earth of the same *Ego* a night on the stage of a theater. One night the actor, or "Ego," appears as "Macbeth," the next as "Shylock," the third as "Romeo," the fourth as "Hamlet" or "King Lear," and so on, until he has run through the whole cycle of <term>incarnations</term> <note>Rebirths; the process of a soul being born into a new physical body.</note>. The Ego begins his life-pilgrimage as a sprite, an "Ariel," or a "Puck"; he plays the part of a *super* <note>original: "super"; short for supernumerary, an actor who has no lines and provides background presence, like an "extra."</note>, is a soldier, a servant, one of the chorus; then he rises to "speaking parts," plays leading *roles*, interspersed with insignificant parts, until he finally retires from the stage as "Prospero," the magician.

ENQ. I understand. You say, then, that this true *Ego* cannot return to earth after death. But surely the actor is at liberty—if he has preserved the sense of his individuality—to return if he likes to the scene of his former actions?

THEO. We say not, simply because such a return to earth would be incompatible with any state of pure, uninterrupted bliss after death.

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...as I am prepared to prove. We say that humans suffer so much undeserved misery during their lives—through the fault of others they are associated with, or because of their environment—that they are surely entitled to perfect rest and peace, if not total bliss, before resuming the burden of life once more. However, we can discuss this in detail later.

### *WHY IS THEOSOPHY ACCEPTED?*

ENQUIRER. I understand to a certain extent; but I see that your teachings are far more complicated and <term>metaphysical</term> <note>Relating to abstract reasoning and the fundamental nature of reality, including concepts like being, knowing, and time.</note> than either <term>Spiritualism</term> <note>A 19th-century movement based on the belief that the spirits of the dead can communicate with the living, often through a medium.</note> or current religious thought. Can you tell me, then, what has caused this system of <term>Theosophy</term> <note>A philosophical system that seeks direct knowledge of the divine and the underlying laws of the universe.</note> which you support to arouse so much interest and so much hostility at the same time?

THEOSOPHIST. There are several reasons for it, I believe. Among other causes that may be mentioned are:

*Firstly*, the great reaction against the bluntly <term>materialistic</term> <note>The philosophical theory that nothing exists except matter and its movements.</note> theories now popular among scientific teachers.

*Secondly*, a general dissatisfaction with the artificial <term>theology</term> <note>The study of religious beliefs and the nature of God.</note> of the various Christian Churches and the growing number of conflicting <term>sects</term> <note>Specific religious groups or denominations, often with differing interpretations of faith.</note>.

*Thirdly*, an ever-growing realization that creeds which are so obviously self-contradictory and which contradict each other cannot be true, and that claims which cannot be verified cannot be real. This natural distrust of conventional religions is only strengthened by their complete failure to preserve morals or to improve the condition of society and the general public.

*Fourthly*, a conviction held by many, and certain knowledge held by a few, that there must be a philosophical and religious system somewhere which is both scientific...

<summary>The author argues that the hardships of life entitle the soul to a period of rest and explains that Theosophy is gaining traction as a reaction against materialistic science and failing institutional religions.</summary>

<keywords>Theosophy, materialism, religious doubt, suffering, reincarnation, spiritual rest, Christian theology</keywords>

<page-num>36</page-num>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...and not merely speculative. *Finally*, a belief, perhaps, that such a system must be sought for in teachings far antedating <note>original: "antedating"; meaning: preceding or coming before in time</note> any modern faith.

ENQUIRER. But how did this system come to be put forward just now?

THEOSOPHIST. Just because the time was found to be ripe. This fact is shown by the determined effort of so many earnest students to reach the *truth*, at whatever cost and wherever it may be concealed. Seeing this, its custodians permitted that some portions, at least, of that truth should be proclaimed. Had the formation of the Theosophical Society been postponed a few years longer, one half of the civilized nations would have become by this time utter <term>materialists</term> <note>Those who believe that nothing exists except matter and its movements.</note>, and the other half <term>anthropomorphists</term> <note>Those who attribute human characteristics or a human form to a god.</note> and <term>phenomenalists</term> <note>In this context, people focused primarily on psychic phenomena or spiritualist "miracles" rather than spiritual philosophy.</note>.

ENQUIRER. Are we to regard Theosophy in any way as a revelation?

THEOSOPHIST. In no way whatever—not even in the sense of a new and direct disclosure from some higher, supernatural, or, at least, *superhuman beings*. It is a revelation only in the sense of an "unveiling" of old, very old, truths to minds that were previously ignorant of them—ignorant even of the existence and preservation of any such <term>archaic knowledge</term> <note>Ancient or extremely old knowledge.</note>.*

---

* It has become "fashionable," especially lately, to mock the notion that there ever was anything but priestly deception in the mysteries of great and civilized peoples, such as the Egyptians, Greeks, or Romans. Even the <term>Rosicrucians</term> <note>A secret brotherhood of sages claiming to possess esoteric wisdom from ancient times.</note> were treated as no better than half-lunatics and half-crooks. Numerous books have been written on them; and novices, who had hardly heard the name a few years before, set themselves up as profound critics and <term>Gnostics</term> <note>Relating to Gnosticism, a collection of ancient religious ideas centered on secret knowledge (gnosis).</note> on the subject of <term>alchemy</term> <note>An ancient practice focused on the transformation of matter, often associated with turning lead into gold or seeking eternal life.</note>, the "fire-philosophers," and mysticism in general. Yet a long series of the <term>Hierophants</term> <note>original: "Hierophants"; high priests or officials who explained religious mysteries.</note> of Egypt, India, <term>Chaldea</term> <note>An ancient region in southern Mesopotamia.</note>, and Arabia are known—along with the greatest philosophers and sages of Greece and the West—to have included all knowledge under the designation of wisdom and divine science; for they considered the...

<sig>D</sig>

**ENQUIRER.** You spoke of "persecution." If truth is as Theosophy represents it, why has it met with such opposition and failed to gain general acceptance?

**THEOSOPHIST.** For many and various reasons again. One of these is the hatred people feel for "innovations," as they call them. Selfishness is fundamentally conservative and hates being disturbed. It prefers an easy-going, undemanding *lie* to the greatest truth, if that truth requires the sacrifice of even one's smallest comfort. The power of mental inertia is strong in anything that does not promise immediate benefit and reward. Our age is pre-eminently unspiritual and literal. 

Furthermore, there is the unfamiliar character of Theosophical teachings and the highly <term>abstruse</term> <note>original: "abstruse"; meaning: extremely difficult to understand.</note> nature of the doctrines. Some of these flatly contradict many of the human <term>vagaries</term> <note>original: "vagaries"; meaning: unpredictable or eccentric ideas.</note> cherished by <term>sectarians</term> <note>Members of a specific religious group, often implied here to be narrow-minded.</note>—ideas which have eaten into the very core of popular beliefs. If we add to this the personal efforts and great purity of life demanded of those who would become disciples of the *inner circle*, and the very limited class of people to whom an entirely unselfish code appeals, it is easy to see why Theosophy is destined for such slow, up-hill work. It is essentially the philosophy of those who suffer and have lost all hope of being helped out of the mire of life by any other means. Moreover, the history of any system of belief or morals newly introduced into a foreign soil shows that its beginnings were impeded by...

---

...foundation and origin of every art and science as essentially divine. Plato regarded the <term>mysteries</term> <note>Secret religious rituals of ancient Greece that were intended to provide spiritual enlightenment.</note> as most sacred, and **Clement of Alexandria** <note>original: "Clemens Alexandrinus"; an early Christian theologian and philosopher (c. 150 – c. 215 AD).</note>, who had been himself initiated into the **Eleusinian mysteries** <note>The most famous of the secret religious rites of ancient Greece, centered on the myth of Demeter and Persephone.</note>, has declared "that the doctrines taught therein contained the end of all human knowledge." Were Plato and Clement two scoundrels <note>original: "knaves"</note> or two fools, we wonder—or both?

...every obstacle that <term>obscurantism</term> <note>original: "obscurantism"; the practice of deliberately preventing the facts or full details of something from becoming known.</note> and selfishness could suggest. "The crown of the innovator is a crown of thorns" indeed! No pulling down of old, worm-eaten buildings can be accomplished without some danger.

**ENQUIRER.** All this refers more to the ethics and philosophy of the <term>T.S.</term> <note>Theosophical Society.</note> Can you give me a general idea of the Society itself, its objects, and its statutes?

**THEOSOPHIST.** This was never kept secret. Ask, and you will receive accurate answers.

**ENQUIRER.** But I heard that you were bound by pledges?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Only in the *Arcane* or "Esoteric" Section.

**ENQUIRER.** And also, I heard that some members, after leaving, did not regard themselves as bound by them. Are they right?

**THEOSOPHIST.** This shows that their idea of honor is an imperfect one. How can they be right? As it was well said in *The Path* (our Theosophical journal in New York) when treating such a case: 

> "Suppose that a soldier is tried for breaking his oath and discipline, and is dismissed from the service. In his rage at the justice he has brought upon himself, and of whose penalties he was clearly forewarned, the soldier turns to the enemy with false information—acting as a spy and traitor—to get revenge upon his former commander. He then claims that his punishment has released him from his oath of loyalty to the cause." 

Do you think he is justified? Don’t you think he deserves to be called a dishonorable man and a coward?

**ENQUIRER.** I believe so; but some think otherwise.

**THEOSOPHIST.** So much the worse for them. But we will talk on this subject later, if you please.

# III.

## THE WORKING SYSTEM OF THE T.S.*

---

->*THE OBJECTS OF THE SOCIETY.*<-

**ENQUIRER.** What are the objects of the "Theosophical Society"?

**THEOSOPHIST.** There are three, and they have been the same from the beginning. (1.) To form the nucleus of a Universal Brotherhood of Humanity without distinction of race, color, or creed. (2.) To promote the study of <term>Aryan</term> <note>In the late 19th century, "Aryan" was a scholarly term used to describe Indo-European languages and the ancient cultures of India and Iran; it did not have the racial or political connotations it acquired in the 20th century.</note> and other scriptures, of the world's religions and sciences, and to demonstrate the importance of ancient Asiatic literature—namely, the <term>Brahmanical</term> <note>Relating to the ancient Vedic traditions of India and the precursor to modern Hinduism.</note>, Buddhist, and <term>Zoroastrian</term> <note>The ancient religion of Persia (modern Iran) founded by the prophet Zoroaster.</note> philosophies. (3.) To investigate the hidden mysteries of nature from every possible perspective, and especially the psychic and spiritual powers <term>latent</term> <note>Existing but not yet developed or manifest; hidden.</note> in humanity. These are, broadly stated, the three chief objects of the Theosophical Society.

**ENQUIRER.** Can you give me some more detailed information about these?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We may divide each of the three objects into as many explanatory clauses as may be found necessary.

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQUIRER.** Then let us begin with the first object. What methods would you use in order to promote such a feeling of brotherhood among races that are known to have the most diverse religions, customs, beliefs, and ways of thinking?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Allow me to add what you seem hesitant to say. Of course, we know that with the exception of two remnants of ancient races—the **Parsees** <note>The Zoroastrian community in India, descended from Persian refugees who fled Islamic persecution in the 7th–10th centuries.</note> and the **Jews**—every nation is divided, not only against all other nations but even against itself. This is most obvious among the so-called "civilized" Christian nations. This is why you are surprised, and why our first goal seems like a <term>Utopia</term> <note>An imaginary, perfect society that is often considered impossible to achieve or unrealistic.</note> to you. Is that not right?

**ENQUIRER.** Well, yes; but what do you have to say about that fact?

**THEOSOPHIST.** I have nothing to say against the fact itself; but I have much to say about the necessity of removing the causes that make Universal Brotherhood a Utopia at present.

**ENQUIRER.** In your view, what are these causes?

**THEOSOPHIST.** First and foremost, the natural selfishness of human nature. Instead of being rooted out, this selfishness is daily strengthened and stimulated into a fierce and unstoppable feeling by current religious education, which tends not only to encourage but to positively justify it. People’s ideas about right and wrong have been entirely distorted by a literal acceptance of the Jewish Bible <note>The Old Testament.</note>. All the unselfishness of the **altruistic** <note>Selfless; showing a disinterested concern for the well-being of others.</note> teachings of Jesus has become merely a theoretical subject for church sermons; meanwhile, the rules of practical selfishness taught in the <term>Mosaic Bible</term> <note>The books of the Old Testament traditionally attributed to Moses, containing the ancient Jewish Law.</note>—which Christ preached against so unsuccessfully—have become deeply ingrained in the inner lives of Western nations. “An eye for an eye..."

### THE COMMON ORIGIN OF HUMANITY <note>original: "MAN" — used here in the universal sense of all humankind.</note>

**ENQUIRER.** How?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Simply by demonstrating on logical, philosophical, metaphysical, and even scientific grounds that:
(a) All people have the same spiritual and physical origin, which is the fundamental teaching of Theosophy.
(b) As mankind is essentially of one and the same essence, and that essence is one—infinite, uncreated, and eternal, whether we call it God or Nature—nothing, therefore, can affect one nation or one person without affecting all other nations and all other people. This is as certain and as obvious as the fact that a stone thrown into a pond will, sooner or later, set in motion every single drop of water within it.

**ENQUIRER.** But this is not the teaching of Christ, but rather a <term>pantheistic</term> <note>The belief that God and the universe are one and the same, rather than God being a separate, personal creator.</note> notion.

**THEOSOPHIST.** That is where your mistake lies. It is purely *Christian*, although *not Judaic* <note>Refers to the religious laws and traditions of Judaism, specifically those in the Old Testament as opposed to the New Testament.</note>, and therefore, perhaps, your Biblical nations prefer to ignore it.

**ENQUIRER.** This is a wholesale and unjust accusation. Where are your proofs for such a statement?

**THEOSOPHIST.** They are ready at hand. Christ is alleged to have said: "Love each other" and "Love your enemies"; for "if you love...

**ENQ.** I have heard you say that science proves we share the same physical origin, while the Wisdom-Religion <note>Theosophy, which claims to be the underlying truth behind all religions.</note> proves we share the same spiritual origin. Yet we do not find Darwinists <note>Followers of Charles Darwin’s theory of evolution, which was still relatively new and controversial when this was written in 1889.</note> exhibiting great brotherly affection.

The identity of our physical origin makes no appeal to our higher and deeper feelings. Matter, when stripped of its soul and spirit—its divine essence—cannot speak to the human heart. But the identity of the soul and spirit of the real, immortal human being, as Theosophy teaches us, would lead us far on the road of real charity and brotherly goodwill once it is proven and deep-rooted in our hearts.

**ENQ.** But how does Theosophy explain the common origin of humanity?

**THEO.** By teaching that the root of all nature, both objective and subjective, and everything else in the universe, visible and invisible, *is, was*, and *ever will be* one absolute essence, from which everything starts and into which everything returns. This is Indo-Aryan philosophy <note>original: "Aryan philosophy." In this context, it refers to the ancient Sanskrit-speaking cultures of India and their philosophical lineage.</note>, which is fully represented today only by the Vedantins <note>Followers of Vedanta, a major school of Hindu philosophy focused on the relationship between the individual soul and the ultimate reality.</note>.

---

> ...were sent to nameless misery in the mines, or under the whip in the cane and rice fields. The guilt for this great crime rests on the Christian Church. "In the name of the most Holy Trinity," the Spanish Government (Roman Catholic) concluded more than ten treaties authorizing the sale of five hundred thousand human beings. In 1562, Sir John Hawkins <note>An English naval commander and the first English merchant to profit from the Atlantic slave trade.</note> sailed on his diabolical errand of buying slaves in Africa and selling them in the West Indies in a ship which bore the sacred name of *Jesus*. Meanwhile, Elizabeth, the Protestant Queen, rewarded him for his success in this first English adventure in that inhuman traffic by allowing him to wear a specific crest on his coat of arms: "a half-figure of a Moor <note>original: "demi-Moor." A "Moor" was a historical term for North African or dark-skinned people.</note> in his natural color, bound with a cord"—or, in other words, a handcuffed African slave. — *Conquests of the Cross* (quoted from the *Agnostic Journal*).

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...them has the *whole* truth, but that they are mutually complementary, that the complete truth can be found only in the combined views of all, after what is false in each of them has been sifted out—then true brotherhood in religion will be established. The same applies in the physical world.

**ENQ.** Please explain further.

**THEO.** Take an instance. A plant consists of a root, a stem, and many shoots and leaves. Just as humanity, as a whole, is the stem which grows from the spiritual root, so is the stem the unity of the plant. Hurt the stem and it is obvious that every shoot and leaf will suffer. So it is with mankind.

**ENQ.** Yes, but if you injure a leaf or a shoot, you do not injure the whole plant.

**THEO.** And therefore you think that by injuring one man you do not injure humanity? But how do you know? Are you aware that even materialistic science <note>original: "materialistic science." A branch of science that focuses exclusively on physical matter and observable phenomena, often excluding spiritual or metaphysical explanations.</note> teaches that any injury, however slight, to a plant will affect the whole course of its future growth and development? Therefore, you are mistaken, and the analogy is perfect. If, however, you overlook the fact that a cut in the finger may often make the whole body suffer, and react on the whole nervous system, I must all the more remind you that there may well be other spiritual laws, operating on plants and animals as well as on mankind; although, as you do not recognize their action on plants and animals, you may deny their existence.

**ENQ.** What laws do you mean?

**THEO.** We call them <term>Karmic laws</term> <note>Laws of cause and effect, where every action has a corresponding consequence that shapes future existence.</note>; but you will not understand the full meaning of the term unless you study <term>Occultism</term> <note>The study of the hidden or "occult" aspects of nature and the human mind that are not visible to the ordinary physical senses.</note>.

<summary>The author uses a plant analogy to illustrate the interconnectedness of humanity, arguing that an injury to one individual affects the whole, and introduces the concept of Karmic laws.</summary>
<keywords>Brotherhood, Unity of Humanity, Materialistic Science, Karmic Laws, Occultism, Plant Analogy, Interconnectedness</keywords>

--- Page 65 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that humanity is an interconnected whole, where the suffering or injury of one individual eventually impacts the entire collective, much like a single plant.</meta>

<page-num>47</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

However, my argument did not rely on the assumption of these laws, but rather on the analogy of the plant. Expand this idea, apply it universally, and you will soon find that in true philosophy, every physical action has a moral and everlasting effect. If you hurt a man by doing him bodily harm, you may think that his pain and suffering cannot spread to his neighbors, and certainly not to people of other nations. We affirm that *it will*, in due time. Therefore, we say that unless every person is brought to understand and accept as an <term>axiomatic truth</term> <note>original: "axiomatic truth." A self-evident principle that requires no proof.</note> that by wronging one man we wrong not only ourselves but the whole of humanity in the long run, no brotherly feelings—such as those preached by all the great Reformers, most notably by Buddha and Jesus—are possible on earth.

### OUR OTHER OBJECTS.

**ENQ.** Will you now explain the methods by which you propose to carry out the second object of the Society?

**THEO.** To collect for the library at our headquarters in Adyar, Madras (and for the local libraries of our branches, through their members <note>original: "Fellows." This refers to the formal members of the Theosophical Society.</note>), all the quality works on the world’s religions that we can find. We also aim to put into written form accurate information about various ancient philosophies, traditions, and legends. We spread <note>original: "disseminate"</note> this information in practical ways, such as the translation and publication of valuable original works, including extracts and commentaries on them, or through the oral instructions of people who are learned in their respective fields.

<summary>Blavatsky argues that harming any individual ultimately harms all of humanity and outlines the Society's mission to collect and distribute ancient religious and philosophical knowledge.</summary>
<keywords>interconnectedness, moral cause and effect, universal brotherhood, Buddha, Jesus, Adyar Library, ancient philosophy, translation, Theosophical Society</keywords>

--- Page 66 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the objects of the Theosophical Society, specifically moving from the study of ancient philosophies to the development of human potential and the Society's ethical foundation.</meta>

<page-num>48</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQ.** And what about the third object: to develop the hidden <note>original: "latent."</note> spiritual or psychic powers within humanity?

**THEO.** This must also be achieved through publications in those places where lectures and personal teachings are not possible. Our duty is to keep a person’s spiritual intuitions alive. We must oppose and counteract—after investigating and proving its irrational nature—bigotry in every form: religious, scientific, or social. Above all, we must oppose *hypocrisy* <note>original: "cant." In this context, it refers to sanctimonious or insincere talk, especially regarding moral or religious matters.</note>, whether it appears as religious narrow-mindedness <note>original: "sectarianism."</note> or as a belief in miracles or anything supernatural. Our task is to seek knowledge of all the laws of nature and to spread <note>original: "diffuse."</note> it. We encourage the study of those laws least understood by modern people, the so-called <term>Occult Sciences</term> <note>original: "Occult Sciences." These were considered the study of the "hidden" or "inner" laws of nature and the mind, which the author argues are based on natural law rather than miracles.</note>, *which are based on a true knowledge of nature instead of the current superstitious beliefs founded on blind faith and authority*. Popular folklore and traditions, however fanciful they may seem at times, may lead to the discovery of long-lost but important secrets of nature when carefully examined <note>original: "sifted."</note>. The Society, therefore, aims to pursue this line of inquiry in the hope of widening the field of scientific and philosophical observation.

**THEO.** The ethics are already there, ready and clear enough for anyone who would follow them. They are the essence and "cream" of the world's ethics, gathered from the teachings of all the world's...

...great reformers. Therefore, you will find represented in them Confucius and Zoroaster, Laotze and the *Bhagavad Gita* <note>original: "Bhagavat-Gita."</note>, the precepts of Gautama Buddha and Jesus of Nazareth, and the teachings of Hillel and his school, as well as Pythagoras, Socrates, Plato, and their schools.

**ENQ.** Do the members of your Society carry out these teachings <note>original: "precepts."</note>? I have heard of great disagreements <note>original: "dissensions."</note> and quarrels among them.

**THEO.** Very naturally, since although the reform (in its present form) may be called new, the men and women to be reformed are the same human, sinning natures as of old. As already stated, the earnest, hardworking members are few; but many are the sincere and well-intentioned people who try their best to live up to the Society’s ideals and their own. Our duty is to encourage and assist individual members <note>original: "fellows."</note> in intellectual, moral, and spiritual self-improvement, not to blame or condemn those who fail. Strictly speaking, we have no right to refuse admission to anyone—especially in the *Esoteric Section* <term>Esoteric Section</term> <note>The private "inner" circle of the Society intended for more advanced or committed spiritual study and practice.</note> of the Society, where “he who enters is like one newly born.” But if any member—despite his sacred pledges, his word of honor, and his immortal *Self* <note>The capitalized "Self" refers to the divine or higher spiritual essence within a person, rather than the everyday ego.</note>—chooses to continue the vices or defects of his old life alongside the "new man" after that “new birth,” and continues to indulge in them within the Society, then, of course, he is more than likely to be asked to resign and withdraw; or, if he refuses, to be expelled. We have the strictest rules for such emergencies.

**ENQ.** Can some of those rules be mentioned?

**THEO.** They can. To begin with, no member <note>original: "Fellow."</note> in the Society, whether exoteric <term>exoteric</term> <note>Refers to the outer, public-facing part of the Society, accessible to all.</note> or esoteric, has a right to force his personal opinions...

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

upon another member <note>original: "Fellow."</note>. “It is not lawful for *any officer of the Parent Society* to express in public, by word or act, any hostility to, or preference for, any one section <note>A "section" refers to a specific branch or subgroup within the Society.</note>, religious or philosophical, more than another. All have an equal right to have the essential features of their religious belief laid before the tribunal of an impartial world. And no officer of the Society, in his capacity as an officer, has the right to preach his own sectarian views and beliefs to members assembled, except when the meeting consists of his co-religionists. After due warning, violation of this rule shall be punished by suspension or expulsion.” This is one of the offenses in the Society at large. As regards the inner section, now called the *Esoteric Section* <term>Esoteric Section</term>, the following rules have been laid down and adopted as far back as 1880: “No member <note>original: "Fellow."</note> shall put to his selfish use any knowledge communicated to him by any member of the first section (now a higher ‘degree’); violation of the rule being punished by expulsion.” Now, however, before any such knowledge can be shared, the applicant has to bind himself by a solemn oath not to use it for selfish purposes, nor to reveal anything said except by permission.

**ENQUIRER.** But is a man who is expelled, or who resigns from the section, free to reveal anything he may have learned, or to break any clause of the pledge he has taken?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Certainly not. His expulsion or resignation only relieves him from the obligation of obedience to the teacher, and from—

* A “branch,” or lodge, composed solely of co-religionists, or a *branch in foreign lands* <note>original: "branch in partibus." -># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

from the duty of taking an active part in the work of the Society, but certainly not from the sacred pledge of secrecy.

**ENQUIRER.** But is this reasonable and just?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Most certainly. To any man or woman with even the slightest sense of honor, a pledge of secrecy taken on one’s word of honor—and even more so to one’s Higher Self, the God within—is binding until death. Even though a person may leave the Section and the Society, no honorable man or woman would think of attacking or harming a group to which he or she has been so pledged.

**ENQUIRER.** But isn't that going a bit too far?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Perhaps so, according to the low moral standards of the present time. But if a pledge does not bind a person this deeply, what use is a pledge at all? How can anyone expect to be taught secret knowledge if they are free to abandon all their obligations whenever they please? What security, confidence, or trust could ever exist among people if pledges like these had no real binding force? Believe me, the law of retribution (<term>Karma</term>) would very quickly overtake someone who broke their pledge in such a way. Perhaps just as quickly, they would face the contempt of every honorable person, even here on this physical plane. As it was well expressed in the New York magazine "*The Path*," which was just cited on this subject: “*A pledge, once taken, is forever binding in both the moral and the occult <note>In this context, "occult" refers to the hidden or spiritual inner workings of nature.</note> worlds. If we break it once and are punished, that does not justify us in breaking it again; as long as we do, the mighty lever of the Law (of <term>Karma</term>) will continue to react upon us.*” (*The Path*, July, 1889.)

# IV.

## THE RELATIONS OF THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY TO THEOSOPHY.

---

->### *ON SELF-IMPROVEMENT.*<-

**ENQUIRER.** Is moral elevation, then, the principal thing insisted upon in your Society?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Undoubtedly! He who would be a true Theosophist must bring himself to live as one.

**ENQUIRER.** If so, then, as I remarked before, the behavior of some members strangely contradicts <note>original: "belies"</note> this fundamental rule.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Indeed it does. But this cannot be helped among us, any more than among those who call themselves Christians and act like fiends. This is no fault of our statutes and rules, but rather that of human nature. Even in some <term>exoteric</term> <note>Exoteric refers to the outer, public-facing part of a spiritual teaching or organization, in contrast to the "esoteric" or secret inner teachings.</note> public branches, the members pledge themselves on their "Higher Self" to live the life prescribed by Theosophy. They have to bring their *Divine Self* to guide their every thought and action, every day and at every moment of their lives. A true Theosophist ought "to deal justly and walk humbly." <note>This is a reference to the biblical verse Micah 6:8, emphasizing humility and justice as core spiritual virtues.</note>

**ENQUIRER.** What do you mean by this?

<summary>This page discusses the necessity of moral self-improvement for members of the Theosophical Society, emphasizing that while human nature often falls short, a true Theosophist must strive to be guided by their "Higher" or "Divine Self."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the practical application of Theosophy, focusing on the transition from individual self-improvement to the concept of total altruism and service to humanity.</meta>

<page-num>53</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**THEOSOPHIST.** Simply this: the individual self must forget itself for the sake of the many other selves. Let me answer you in the words of a true <term>Philaletheian</term> <note>original: "Philaletheian," meaning a lover of truth, from the Greek "philos" (loving) and "aletheia" (truth).</note>, a <term>Fellow of the Theosophical Society</term> <note>original: "F.T.S."</note>, who has beautifully expressed it in the journal *The Theosophist*: "What every man needs first is to find himself, and then take an honest inventory of his internal <note>original: "subjective"</note> possessions; and, however bad or bankrupt they may be, they are not beyond redemption if we set about the task in earnest." But how many actually do this? All are willing to work for their own development and progress; very few work for the progress of others. To quote the same writer again: "People have been deceived and deluded long enough; they must break their idols, cast aside their pretenses, and go to work for themselves—or rather, there is one word too many there, for he who works only for himself had better not work at all. Instead, let him work for others, for everyone. For every flower of love and charity he plants in his neighbor's garden, a disgusting weed will disappear from his own, and so this 'garden of the gods'—Humanity—shall blossom like a rose. This is plainly set forth in all Bibles and all religions, but calculating <note>original: "designing"</note> men first misinterpreted and finally weakened, commercialized, and muddled <note>original: "emasculated, materialised, besotted"</note> them. It does not require a new revelation. Let every man be a revelation to himself. Once a man's immortal spirit takes possession of the temple of his body, driving out the money-changers <note>A biblical reference to Jesus clearing the Temple of those who profited from religion.</note> and every unclean thing, his own divine humanity will redeem him; for when he is at one with himself in this way, he will know the 'Builder of the Temple.'"

**ENQUIRER.** I admit, this is pure altruism.

**THEOSOPHIST.** It is. And if only one in ten <term>Fellows of the Theosophical Society</term> would practice it, ours would be a truly elite group. But there—

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

There are those among the outsiders who will always refuse to see the essential difference between Theosophy and the Theosophical Society—the difference between the ideal and its imperfect embodiment. Such people would blame every sin and shortcoming of the vehicle (the human body) on the pure spirit that sheds its divine light upon it. Is this fair to either? They throw stones at an association that tries to work toward, and for the spread of, its ideal against overwhelming <note>original: "tremendous"</note> odds. Some slander <note>original: "vilify"</note> the Theosophical Society only because it dares to attempt what other systems—especially Church and State Christianity—have failed at most miserably <note>original: "egregiously"</note>. Others do so because they would prefer to preserve the current state of things: <term>Pharisees and Sadducees</term> <note>Religious groups in the New Testament often depicted as hypocritical or focused on status.</note> in the seat of authority, and <term>publicans and sinners</term> <note>In a Biblical context, these were tax collectors and social outcasts; here used to describe corrupt people in power.</note> reveling in high places, just as in the Roman Empire during its decline. 

Fair-minded people, at any rate, ought to remember that the person who does all they can does as much as the person who has achieved the most in this world of relative possibilities. This is a simple truth—an axiom supported for believers in the Gospels by the <term>parable of the talents</term> <note>A Biblical story from Matthew 25 where servants are rewarded based on how they managed what they were given.</note> told by their Master: the servant who doubled his *two* talents was rewarded as much as the other fellow servant who had received *five*. To every man, it is given “according to his own ability.”

**ENQ.** Yet it is rather difficult to draw the line between the abstract and the concrete in this case, as we have only the latter by which to form our judgment.

**THEO.** Then why make an exception for the T.S. <note>The Theosophical Society.</note>? Justice, like charity, ought to begin at home. Will you mock and insult the “Sermon on the Mount” because your social and political...

Moreover, if Western Theosophists experience so much difficulty in leading the true Theosophical life...

### *THE ABSTRACT AND THE CONCRETE.*

**ENQUIRER.** Please clarify this difference a little more.

**THEOSOPHIST.** The Society is a large group of men and women made up of very diverse <note>original: "heterogeneous"</note> individuals. Theosophy, in its abstract meaning, is Divine Wisdom, or the total sum of the knowledge and wisdom that underlies the Universe—the unified essence of eternal GOOD. In its concrete sense, it is the sum total of that same wisdom as it is granted to humanity by nature on this earth, and nothing more. Some members earnestly try to realize and, so to speak, give physical form to Theosophy in their lives. Others only want to know about it, not practice it. Still others may have joined the Society merely out of curiosity, a passing interest, or perhaps because some of their friends belong to it. How, then, can the system be judged by the standards of those who claim the name without any right to it? Should poetry or its muse be measured only by those aspiring poets who offend our ears? The Society can be seen as the embodiment of Theosophy only in its abstract motives; it can never claim to be its...

...concrete vehicle as long as human imperfections and weaknesses are all represented in its body. Theosophy is divine nature, both visible and invisible, and its Society is human nature trying to ascend to its divine parent. Finally, Theosophy is the fixed eternal sun, and its Society is the fleeting <note>original: "evanescent"</note> comet trying to settle into an orbit to become a planet, forever revolving within the attraction of the sun of truth. It was formed to help show people that such a thing as Theosophy exists, and to help them ascend toward it by studying and internalizing its eternal truths <note>original: "verities"</note>.

**ENQUIRER.** I thought you said you had no tenets or doctrines of your own?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We do not. The Society has no wisdom of its own to support or teach. It is simply the storehouse of all the truths uttered by the great seers, initiates <note>Those who have been admitted into secret or sacred knowledge</note>, and prophets of historic and even prehistoric ages—at least, as many as it can obtain. Therefore, it is merely the channel through which some portion of the truth, found in the accumulated sayings of humanity's great teachers, is poured out into the world.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Not at all. The undeniable existence of great initiates—true "Sons of God" <note>A term used here for spiritually advanced beings or divine teachers found in various traditions</note>—shows that such wisdom was often...

<meta>This page continues the dialogue regarding the nature of Theosophy, moving from the definition of the Theosophical Society to the relationship between the underlying "Divine Wisdom" and the world's various organized religions.</meta>

<page-num>58</page-num>

...reached by isolated individuals, though always under the initial guidance of a master. But most of their followers, when they became masters in their own right, shrunk the universality <note>original: "catholicism"</note> of these teachings into the narrow groove of their own sectarian dogmas. The commandments of a chosen master alone were then adopted and followed to the exclusion of all others—if they are followed at all—note well—as is the case with the Sermon on the Mount. Each religion is thus a small piece of divine truth, used to focus a vast display of human imagination <note>original: "fancy"</note> which claimed to represent and replace that truth.

**ENQUIRER.** But Theosophy, you say, is not a religion?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Most certainly it is not, since it is the essence of all religion and of absolute truth, only a drop of which lies beneath every creed. To use a metaphor once more: on earth, Theosophy is like the white ray of the spectrum, and every religion is only one of the seven colors of the prism. By ignoring all the others and cursing them as false, every specific colored ray claims not only priority, but to be *that white ray* itself. It even condemns <note>original: "anathematizes"</note> its own shades, from light to dark, as heresies. Yet, as the sun of truth rises higher and higher on the horizon of human perception, and each colored ray gradually fades out until it is finally absorbed back into the source, humanity will at last no longer be cursed with artificial polarizations. Instead, it will find itself bathing in the pure, colorless sunlight of eternal truth. And this will be *Theosophia* <note>Greek: "Divine Wisdom"</note>.

**ENQUIRER.** Your claim is, then, that all the great religions are derived from Theosophy, and that it is by incorporating it that the world will be finally saved from the curse of its great illusions and errors?

<summary>This page explains that while individual religions are narrow, sectarian interpretations of truth, Theosophy represents the "white ray" or universal source from which all religions (the "prismatic colors") originate.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, universal truth, sectarianism, religion vs. philosophy, spectrum metaphor, Theosophia, Sermon on the Mount, dogma</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue about the purpose of the Theosophical Society, shifting from the metaphor of light and prisms to the organic metaphor of a seed growing into the Tree of Knowledge.</meta>

<page-num>59</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**THEOSOPHIST.** Precisely so. And we add that our Theosophical Society is the humble seed which, if watered and left to live, will finally produce the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, which is grafted onto the Tree of Life Eternal <note>Blavatsky uses these biblical symbols to represent the evolution of human consciousness and spiritual immortality</note>. For it is only by studying the various great religions and philosophies of humanity, by comparing them dispassionately and with an unbiased mind, that people can hope to arrive at the truth. It is especially by finding out and noting their various points of agreement that we may achieve this result. For no sooner do we arrive—either by study, or by being taught by someone who knows—at their inner meaning, than we find, almost in every case, that it expresses some great truth in Nature.

**ENQUIRER.** We have heard of a Golden Age <note>A mythological era of peace and harmony</note> that once existed, and what you describe would be a Golden Age to be realized at some future day. When shall it be?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Not before humanity, as a whole, feels the need for it. A maxim in the Persian *Javidan Khirad* <note>original: "Javidan Khirad" (Persian: "Eternal Wisdom"), a 9th-century work of moral philosophy</note> says: "Truth is of two kinds—one manifest and self-evident; the other demanding incessantly new demonstrations and proofs." It is only when this latter kind of truth becomes as universally obvious as it is now dim, and therefore liable to be distorted by sophistry <note>the use of clever but false arguments</note> and casuistry <note>the use of clever but unsound reasoning, especially in moral matters</note>; it is only when the two kinds will have become once more one, that all people will be brought to see alike.

**ENQUIRER.** But surely those few who have felt the need of such truths must have made up their minds to believe in something definite? You tell me that, the Society having no doctrines of its own, every member may believe as he chooses and accept what he pleases. This looks as if the Theosophical Society was bent upon...

<summary>The Theosophist explains that the Society aims to find universal truth by comparing religions and philosophies, looking forward to a future Golden Age when truth is self-evident to all humanity. The dialogue touches on the importance of finding common ground in spiritual traditions and the challenges of deceptive reasoning.</summary>

<keywords>Theosophical Society, Tree of Knowledge, Tree of Life, comparative religion, Golden Age, Javidan Khirad, Eternal Wisdom, sophistry, casuistry</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue regarding the diversity of beliefs within the Theosophical Society. The Enquirer asks if there is any shared foundation to prevent ideological confusion.</meta>

<page-num>60</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...[will be combined that we shall avoid] reviving the confusion of languages and beliefs like the Tower of Babel <note>A biblical story explaining why humanity speaks different languages and lacks a single understanding</note> of old. Do you have no beliefs in common?

**THEOSOPHIST.** What is meant by the Society having no tenets <note>original: "tenets"; principles or beliefs</note> or doctrines of its own is that no specific doctrines or beliefs are mandatory for its members. However, this applies only to the organization as a whole. As you were told, the Society is divided into an outer and an inner group. Those who belong to the latter do, of course, have a philosophy—or a religious system, if you prefer—of their own.

**ENQUIRER.** May we be told what it is?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We make no secret of it. It was outlined a few years ago in the journal *The Theosophist* and the book *Esoteric Buddhism* <note>A book by A.P. Sinnett published in 1883 that introduced many Westerners to Theosophical concepts</note>, and it can be found in even greater detail in *The Secret Doctrine* <note>Helena Blavatsky's foundational work, published in 1888, which outlines the movement's cosmology and philosophy</note>. It is based on the oldest philosophy in the world, known as the "Wisdom-Religion" or the "Ancient Doctrine." If you like, you may ask questions and have them explained.

<summary>This page clarifies that while the Theosophical Society does not require its general members to hold specific beliefs, its inner circle follows a specific philosophy called the "Wisdom-Religion." This ancient system is detailed in foundational texts like The Secret Doctrine and Esoteric Buddhism.</summary>

# V.

## THE FUNDAMENTAL TEACHINGS OF THEOSOPHY.

---

### *ON GOD AND PRAYER.*

**ENQUIRER.** Do you believe in God?

**THEOSOPHIST.** That depends on what you mean by that term.

**ENQUIRER.** I mean the Christian God, the Father of Jesus and the Creator—the biblical God of Moses, in short.

**THEOSOPHIST.** We do not believe in such a God. We reject the idea of a personal, or an "extra-cosmic" <note>Existing outside of or separate from the physical universe</note> and <term>anthropomorphic</term> <note>Attributing human characteristics, forms, or personality to a non-human being or God</note> God, who is nothing more than a giant shadow of *man*—and not even man at his best. The God of traditional theology, we argue—and can prove—is a bundle of contradictions and a logical impossibility. Therefore, we will have nothing to do with him.

**ENQUIRER.** Please explain your reasons.

**THEOSOPHIST.** There are many reasons, and we cannot address them all here. But here are a few. This God is described by his followers as being infinite and absolute, is he not?

**ENQUIRER.** Yes, I believe he is.

<summary>In this opening dialogue on Theosophical teachings, the Theosophist explains the movement's rejection of a personal, human-like, or "extra-cosmic" God. They argue that the traditional theological definition of God is logically contradictory and inconsistent with the concepts of infinity and the absolute.</summary>

<keywords>God, theology, anthropomorphic, extra-cosmic, infinite, absolute, biblical God, logic, Theosophical teachings</keywords>

--- Page 80 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the logical contradictions of a personal, finite God versus the Theosophical concept of an Absolute Principle.</meta>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**THEOSOPHIST.** Then, if he is infinite—that is, limitless—and especially if he is absolute, how can he have a form or be the creator of anything? Form implies limitation, as well as a beginning and an end. In order to create, a Being must think and plan. How can the ABSOLUTE be supposed to think—that is, to have any relation at all to that which is limited, finite, and conditioned? This is a philosophical and logical absurdity. Even the Hebrew <term>Kabala</term> <note>A system of Jewish mysticism</note> rejects such an idea. Therefore, it describes the one and Absolute Divine Principle as an infinite Unity called <term>Ain-Soph</term>.* In order to create, a Creator must become active; and since this is impossible for ABSOLUTENESS, the infinite principle had to be shown becoming the cause of evolution (not creation) in an indirect way—that is, through the emanation from itself of the <term>Sephiroth</term> <note>The ten attributes or emanations in the Kabala through which the Infinite reveals itself</note>. (This idea of emanation is another absurdity, though this time it is due to the translators of the <term>Kabala</term>.)†

**ENQUIRER.** What about those <term>Kabalists</term> who, while following these teachings, still believe in Jehovah, or the *Tetragrammaton* <note>The four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH</note>?

**THEOSOPHIST.** They are free to believe in whatever they please, as their belief or disbelief can hardly affect a self-evident fact. The Jesuits tell us that two and two are not always four to a—

***

\* <term>Ain-Soph</term>, <note>Hebrew: אֵין סוֹף, *Ein Sof*</note> = the All <note>original Greek: τὸ πᾶν, *to pan*</note> = infinite <note>original Greek: ἄπειρος, *apeiros*</note>. This represents the endless or boundless reality within Nature—the "non-existent" which *IS*, but is not a specific *Being*.

† How can the non-active eternal principle emanate or emit? The <term>Parabrahm</term> <note>In Hindu philosophy, the "Supreme Brahman" or absolute reality</note> of the <term>Vedantins</term> <note>Followers of the Vedanta school of Indian philosophy</note> does nothing of the kind; nor does the <term>Ain-Soph</term> of the Chaldean <term>Kabala</term>. It is an eternal and periodical law which causes an active and creative force (the <term>logos</term> <note>Greek for "word" or "reason," used here as a creative principle</note>) to emerge from the ever-concealed and incomprehensible one principle at the beginning of every <term>maha-manvantara</term> <note>Sanskrit: a "great cycle" or long period of universal activity</note>, or new cycle of life.

<summary>The Theosophist argues that an "Absolute" God cannot be a "Creator" because creation requires thought and action, which are limitations. They explain that Theosophy and the Kabala instead view the universe as an indirect evolution from an infinite, inactive principle through periodic natural laws.</summary>

<keywords>Kabala, Ain-Soph, Sephiroth, Absolute, evolution vs creation, Tetragrammaton, Parabrahm, maha-manvantara, logos, philosophy of religion</keywords>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...certainty, since it depends on the will of God to make 2 × 2 = 5. Shall we accept their <term>sophistry</term> <note>original: "sophistry"; clever but false reasoning intended to deceive</note> for all that?

**ENQUIRER.** Then you are atheists?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Not that we know of, and not unless the label of "Atheist" is to be applied to those who disbelieve in an <term>anthropomorphic God</term> <note>A god conceived as having a human form or human personality traits</note>. We believe in a <term>Universal Divine Principle</term>, the root of ALL, from which everything proceeds, and within which everything shall be absorbed at the end of the great <term>cycle of Being</term> <note>The vast period of cosmic existence before the universe returns to a state of rest</note>.

**ENQUIRER.** This is the old, old claim of <term>Pantheism</term> <note>The belief that the universe is identical with divinity, or that everything composes an all-encompassing, immanent God</note>. If you are <term>Pantheists</term>, you cannot be <term>Deists</term> <note>People who believe in a creator who does not intervene in the universe</note>; and if you are not Deists, then you have to answer to the name of Atheists.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Not necessarily so. The term "Pantheism" is again one of the many abused terms whose real and original meaning has been distorted by blind prejudice and a one-sided view. If you accept the Christian <term>etymology</term> <note>The study of the origin of words</note> of this compound word, and form it from *pan*, meaning "all" <note>original Greek: πᾶν</note>, and *theos*, meaning "god" <note>original Greek: θεὸς</note>, and then imagine and teach that this means that every stone and every tree in Nature is a God or the ONE God, then, of course, you will be right. You would then turn Pantheists into <term>fetish-worshippers</term> <note>original: "fetish-worshippers"; those who worship inanimate objects believed to have magical powers</note>, in addition to their legitimate name. But you will hardly be as successful if you define the word Pantheism <term>esoterically</term> <note>According to specialized, hidden, or inner knowledge</note>, as we do.

**ENQUIRER.** What is, then, your definition of it?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Let me ask you a question in my turn. What do you understand by Pan, or Nature?

<summary>The Theosophist clarifies that they are not atheists, but rather believers in a Universal Divine Principle. They argue that Pantheism is often misunderstood by critics as the worship of physical objects, whereas Theosophy defines it through a deeper, esoteric lens.</summary>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQUIRER.** Nature is, I suppose, the sum total of things existing around us; the aggregate of causes and effects in the world of matter—the creation or universe.

**THEOSOPHIST.** So, you mean the personified sum and order of known causes and effects? The total of all finite agencies and forces, viewed as being utterly disconnected from an intelligent Creator or Creators, and perhaps "conceived of as a single and separate force"—as described in your encyclopedias?

**ENQUIRER.** Yes, I believe so.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Well, we do not take into consideration this objective and material nature, which we call an evanescent illusion <note>An "evanescent illusion" refers to the idea that the material world is temporary and constantly changing, unlike the eternal spiritual reality</note>. Nor do we mean by "all" <note>original Greek: πᾶν (*pan*)</note> Nature in the sense of its accepted derivation from the Latin *Natura* (meaning "becoming," from the root *nasci*, "to be born"). When we speak of the Deity and make it identical—and therefore <term>coeval</term> <note>Existing at the same time; having the same age or duration</note>—with Nature, we mean the eternal and uncreated nature, not your collection of flitting shadows and finite unrealities. We leave it to the hymn-makers to call the visible sky or heaven "God's Throne," and our earth of mud "His footstool." Our DEITY is neither in a paradise, nor in a particular tree, building, or mountain. It is everywhere, in every atom of the visible as well as the invisible <term>Cosmos</term> <note>The universe seen as a well-ordered whole</note>; it is in, over, and around every invisible atom and divisible molecule. For IT is the mysterious power of <term>evolution and involution</term> <note>"Evolution" here refers to the outward manifestation of spirit into matter, while "involution" is the inward withdrawal of matter back into spirit</note>—the omnipresent, omnipotent, and even omniscient creative potentiality.

**ENQUIRER.** Stop! Omniscience <note>The state of knowing everything</note> is the prerogative of something that thinks, and you deny to your "Absoluteness" the power of thought.

**THEOSOPHIST.** We deny it to the **ABSOLUTE** <note>In philosophy, the Absolute is the ultimate, unconditioned reality that exists outside of time, space, and human thought</note>, since thought is something limited and conditioned. But you evidently forget that in

<summary>This page distinguishes between the material, "illusory" nature studied by science and the eternal, uncreated "Nature" identified with the Deity in Theosophy. The Theosophist argues that the Divine is an omnipresent potentiality rather than a thinking, personal being.</summary>

# 65

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

In [Theosophical] philosophy, absolute unconsciousness is also absolute consciousness; otherwise, it would not be "absolute." <note>The argument here is that if something is "absolute," it must contain all possibilities, including both consciousness and unconsciousness, without being limited by either.</note>

**ENQUIRER.** Then your Absolute thinks?

**THEOSOPHIST.** No, IT does not, for the simple reason that it is *Absolute Thought* itself. Nor does it "exist," for the same reason—because it is absolute existence, or **Be-ness** <term>Be-ness</term> <note>A term used in Theosophy to describe a state of pure existence that is beyond the limitations of "being" a specific thing or person</note>, rather than a **Being**. Read the superb **Kabbalistic** <term>Kabbalistic</term> <note>Relating to the Kabbalah, a school of Jewish mysticism that explores the nature of the divine and the universe</note> poem by Solomon Ben Judah Gabirol in the *Kether-Malchut* <note>original: *Kether-Malchut*, Hebrew for "The Kingly Crown," a philosophical poem written around 1050 AD</note>, and you will understand:

> "You are one, the root of all numbers, but not as an element of counting; for unity does not allow for multiplication, change, or form. You are one, and in the secret of Your unity the wisest of men are lost, because they do not know it. You are one, and Your unity is never diminished, never extended, and cannot be changed. You are one, and no thought of mine can set a limit for You or define You. You ARE, but not as one who exists [in a material sense], for the understanding and vision of mortals cannot reach Your existence, nor determine for You the 'where,' the 'how,' and the 'why,'" etc., etc.

In short, our Deity is the eternal, incessantly evolving (not "creating") builder of the universe. That universe itself unfolds out of its own essence; it is not "made." Symbolically, it is a sphere without a circumference <note>A reference to a famous mystical definition: "God is a circle whose center is everywhere and whose circumference is nowhere."</note>, which has only one ever-acting attribute that embraces all other existing or thinkable attributes—ITSELF. It is the one Law, giving the impulse to visible, eternal, and unchangeable laws. These exist within that never-manifesting—*because* absolute—LAW, which during its active periods is *The Ever-Becoming*.

**ENQUIRER.** I once heard one of your members remarking that the Universal Deity, being everywhere, was in "vessels of dishonor" as well as in those of...

<summary>This page explains the Theosophical concept of the Absolute as "Be-ness" and "Absolute Thought," rather than a personal God who thinks or exists as a separate entity. It uses a medieval Jewish poem to illustrate that the Divine is an infinite, unchanging unity from which the universe naturally evolves.</summary>

**Theosophist.** I do not think so, as simple logic can hardly be regarded as blasphemy. If we were to exclude the **Omnipresent Principle** <term>Omnipresent Principle</term> <note>The idea that the divine essence exists everywhere at all times and in all things.</note> from even a single mathematical point of the universe, or from a particle of matter occupying any conceivable space, could we still regard it as infinite?

### IS IT NECESSARY TO PRAY?

**Enquirer.** Do you believe in prayer, and do you ever pray?

**Theosophist.** We do not. We *act*, instead of *talking*.

**Enquirer.** You do not offer prayers even to the **Absolute Principle**? <term>Absolute Principle</term> <note>In Theosophy, the ultimate, nameless reality from which everything originates; it is not a person or a deity with a personality.</note>

**Theosophist.** Why should we? As people who are well-occupied, we can hardly afford to waste time addressing verbal prayers to a pure abstraction. The **Unknowable** <term>Unknowable</term> <note>A term for the ultimate reality that lies beyond human comprehension.</note> is capable of relationships only between its own parts, but it is non-existent in regards to any finite relations <note>Meaning the Infinite cannot have a personal relationship with a finite human being</note>. The visible universe depends for its existence and phenomena on its mutually acting forms and their laws, not on prayer or petitions.

**Enquirer.** Do you not believe at all in the effectiveness of prayer?

**Theosophist.** Not in prayer taught as a set of words and repeated externally—if by prayer you mean the outward petition to an unknown God as the recipient, which was started by the **Jews** and made popular by the **Pharisees** <term>Pharisees</term> <note>A Jewish sect often used in this context to represent those who focus on outward ritual and formal legalism rather than inner spirituality.</note>.

**Enquirer.** Is there any other kind of prayer?

<summary>The Theosophist argues that because the Divine is infinite, it must be present in all matter, and rejects outward, verbal prayer in favor of action and adherence to natural laws.</summary>
<keywords>Omnipresence, Absolute Principle, Unknowable, prayer, ritualism, Pharisees, logic vs blasphemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the nature of prayer. Having established that they do not pray to an external deity, the Theosophist now explains the concept of "will-prayer" and the internal "Father in heaven."</meta>

# 67

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**Theosophist.** Most decidedly; we call it **WILL-PRAYER**, and it is more of an internal command than a petition.

**Enquirer.** To whom, then, do you pray when you do so?

**Theosophist.** To "our Father in heaven"—in its **esoteric** <term>esoteric</term> <note>Intended for or understood by only a small number of people with specialized knowledge.</note> meaning.

**Enquirer.** Is that different from the meaning given to it in traditional theology?

**Theosophist.** Entirely so. An **Occultist** <term>Occultist</term> <note>A student of the "hidden" or secret laws of nature and the universe.</note> or a **Theosophist** addresses their prayer to *their Father who is in secret* (read and try to understand Matthew chapter 6, verse 6), not to an **extra-cosmic** <term>extra-cosmic</term> <note>The idea of a God existing outside of or separate from the universe, which Theosophy argues would make that God limited or finite.</note> and therefore finite God; and that "Father" is within the person themselves.

**Enquirer.** Then you turn a human being into a God?

**Theosophist.** Please say "God" and not *a God*. In our view, the inner person is the only God we can have knowledge of. And how can it be otherwise? Grant us our premise that God is a universally diffused, infinite principle—how then can a human alone escape being saturated by and existing *within* the Deity? We call our "Father in heaven" 그 divine essence that we recognize within us, in our heart and spiritual consciousness. This has nothing to do with the **anthropomorphic** <term>anthropomorphic</term> <note>Attributing human characteristics, emotions, or intentions to a god.</note> conception we might form of it in our physical brain or through our imagination: "Do you not know that you are the temple of God, and that the spirit of (the absolute) God dwells in you?" * Yet,

---

* One often finds conflicting statements in Theosophical writings about the **Christos** <term>Christos</term> <note>The Greek term for "Anointed," representing the divine spark or higher self in every human.</note> principle in humans. Some call it the sixth principle (**Buddhi** <term>Buddhi</term> <note>The spiritual soul; the faculty of intuition and discernment.</note>), others the seventh (**Atman** <term>Atman</term> <note>The highest spiritual self; the pure essence of a person that is one with the universe.</note>). If Christian Theosophists wish to make use of such expressions, let them be made philosophically correct by following the analogy of the symbols of the ancient **Wisdom-religion** <term>Wisdom-religion</term> <note>The underlying system of truth that Theosophists believe is the root of all world religions.</note>. We say that **Christos** is not only one of the three higher principles, but all three regarded as a **Trinity**. This Trinity represents the Holy Spirit, the Father, and the Son, just as it corresponds to abstract spirit,

<summary>The Theosophist defines prayer as an internal "will-prayer" directed to the divine essence within the human spirit rather than to an external deity. They argue that because God is infinite and omnipresent, the divine must reside within humanity as the "Father in secret."</summary>

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.

Let no one attribute human characteristics to that essence within us. If a Theosophist wishes to hold to divine rather than human truth, they must not say that this "God in secret" <note>A reference to Matthew 6:6: "pray to thy Father which is in secret."</note> listens to us, or is separate from either finite humanity or the infinite essence—for all are one. Nor, as just remarked, is a prayer a petition. It is a mystery instead; a hidden <term>occult</term> process by which limited and conditioned thoughts and desires—which cannot be absorbed by the absolute, unconditioned spirit—are translated into spiritual intentions and "the will." This process is called "spiritual transmutation" <note>The process of changing one's lower nature into a higher spiritual state, much like an alchemist transforms base metals.</note>. The intensity of our earnest aspirations changes prayer into the "philosopher's stone" <note>In alchemy, a legendary substance that could turn lead into gold; here it represents the power to turn base desires into spiritual wisdom.</note>, or that which transforms lead into pure gold. As the only consistent essence, our "will-prayer" becomes the active or creative force, producing results according to our desire.

**ENQUIRER.** Do you mean to say that prayer is a hidden process that brings about physical results?

**THEOSOPHIST.** I do. **Will-Power** becomes a living power. But woe to those Occultists and Theosophists who, instead of crushing the desires of the **lower personal ego** or physical self, send up waves of will-power for selfish or unholy purposes! They should instead address their **Higher Spiritual Ego**, which is bathed in the light of the **Spirit-Soul** <note>original: *Atma-Buddhic*</note>, and say, "Thy will be done, not mine," and so on. To use this power for selfish ends is black magic, an abomination, and spiritual sorcery.

***

...differentiated spirit and embodied spirit. Krishna and Christ are philosophically the same principle under its triple aspect of manifestation. In the *Bhagavad Gita* <note>An ancient Indian scripture; the name translates to "The Song of the Lord."</note>, we find Krishna calling himself indifferently **the Spirit** <note>original: *Atman*</note>, the abstract Spirit; **the Knower of the Field** <note>original: *Kshetragna*</note>, the **Higher** or **reincarnating Ego**; and the **Universal SELF**. All these names, when applied to a human being rather than the Universe, correspond to the **Spirit**, **Soul**, and **Mind** <note>original: *Atma, Buddhi and Manas*</note>. The *Anugita* <note>A sacred Sanskrit text found within the Mahabharata that further explains the philosophies of the Bhagavad Gita.</note> is full of this same doctrine.

<summary>The author explains that prayer is an internal, creative act of will that transforms human desire into spiritual force, warning that using this power for selfish ends constitutes black magic. The text also identifies Krishna and Christ as representing the same universal spiritual principles within the human constitution.</summary>

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.

Unfortunately, all of this is the favorite occupation of our Christian statesmen and generals—especially when the latter are sending two armies to murder each other. Both indulge in a bit of such sorcery <note>The author equates public prayers for victory in war with "sorcery" because they attempt to use spiritual forces to cause destruction.</note> before battle by offering prayers to the same "God of Hosts" <note>original: *Lord of Sabaoth*, a biblical title for God as a leader of heavenly armies.</note>, each begging for his help to cut the throats of their enemies.

**ENQUIRER.** David prayed to the Lord of Hosts to help him strike down the Philistines and slaughter the Syrians and the Moabites, and "the Lord preserved David wherever he went." In doing this, we are only following what we find in the Bible.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Of course you do. But since you delight in calling yourselves Christians—not Israelites or Jews, as far as we know—why do you not instead follow what Christ says? He distinctly commands you not to follow "those of old times" <note>A reference to Jesus’s Sermon on the Mount, where he contrasts his teachings with older traditions.</note> or the Mosaic Law <note>The religious and moral laws found in the first five books of the Bible, traditionally attributed to Moses.</note>. Instead, he tells you to do as he says and warns those who would kill with the sword that they, too, will perish by the sword. 

Christ has given you one prayer <note>The Lord's Prayer (the "Our Father").</note> which you have turned into a "lip-prayer" <term>lip-prayer</term> and a boast, and which no one but the *true* Occultist understands. In your "dead-letter" <term>dead-letter</term> interpretation, you say: "Forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors"—which you never actually do. Furthermore, he told you to *love your enemies and do good to those who hate you*. It was surely not the "meek prophet of Nazareth" who taught you to pray to your "Father" to slaughter others and grant you victory over your enemies! This is why we reject what you call "prayers."

**ENQUIRER.** But how do you explain the universal fact that all nations and peoples have prayed to and worshiped a God or gods? Some have adored and tried to appease <term>propitiate</term> devils and harmful spirits, but this only proves how universal the belief is in the effectiveness of prayer.
