-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**THEOSOPHIST:** Leave them to their *Karma* <note>original: "Karma"; the spiritual principle of cause and effect where a person's intent and actions influence their future.</note>. Because one person does evil, that is no reason for others to do so.

**ENQUIRER:** But, to return to slander, where is the *boundary* <note>original: "line of demarcation"</note> between backbiting and just criticism to be drawn? Is it not one’s duty to warn one's friends and neighbors against those whom one knows to be dangerous associates?

**THEOSOPHIST:** If by allowing them to go on unchecked other persons may be injured, it is certainly our duty to prevent <note>original: "obviate"</note> the danger by warning them privately. But whether true or false, no accusation against another person should ever be spread abroad. If true, and the fault hurts no one but the sinner, then leave him to his *Karma*. If false, then you will have avoided adding to the injustice in the world. Therefore, keep silent about such things with everyone not directly concerned. But if your discretion and silence are likely to hurt or endanger others, then I add: *Speak the truth at all costs*, and say, with Annesley <note>Samuel Annesley (c. 1620–1696), a prominent nonconformist minister known for his integrity.</note>, "Consult duty, not events." There are cases when one is forced to exclaim, "*Let discretion perish, rather than allow it to interfere with duty.*"

**ENQUIRER:** It seems to me <note>original: "Methinks"</note>, if you carry out these maxims, you are likely to reap a nice crop of troubles!

**THEOSOPHIST:** And so we do. We have to admit that we are now open to the same taunt that the early Christians were. "See how these Theosophists love one another!" <note>A sarcastic play on a famous quote by the early Christian author Tertullian, who reported that pagans looked at Christians and said, "See how they love one another!" Here, it refers to the public infighting among Society members.</note> may now be said of us without a shadow of injustice.

**ENQUIRER:** Admitting yourself that there is at least as much, if not more, backbiting, slandering, and quarreling in the Theosophical Society <note>original: "T. S."</note> as in the...

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

Christian Churches, not to mention Scientific Societies—what kind of Brotherhood is this, I might ask?

**THEOSOPHIST:** A very poor specimen, indeed, as it stands at present; and until it is carefully sifted and reorganized, it is *no* better than any other group. Remember, however, that human nature is the same *inside* the Theosophical Society as it is *outside* of it. Its members are not saints; they are, at best, sinners trying to do better, yet liable to fall back due to personal weakness. Add to this that our "Brotherhood" is not a "recognized" or established body, and stands, so to speak, outside the reach of legal authority <note>original: "pale of jurisdiction"; referring to the Society's lack of formal legal status or state protection at the time.</note>. Besides that, it is in a chaotic state and is more *unjustly unpopular than any other organization*. Is it any wonder, then, that those members who fail to live up to its ideals turn to our enemies for sympathetic protection after leaving the Society? They pour all their resentment <note>original: "gall"</note> and bitterness into ears that are all too willing to listen! Knowing they will find support, sympathy, and easy belief <note>original: "ready credence"</note> for every accusation—no matter how absurd—that they choose to launch against the Theosophical Society, they hasten to do so. They vent their anger on the innocent mirror that reflected their own faces all too faithfully. People never forgive those whom they have *wronged*. The memory of kindness they received, which they repaid with ingratitude, drives them into a frenzy of self-justification before the world and their own consciences. The world <note>referring back to "the world" mentioned in the previous sentence</note> is only too ready to believe anything said against a society it hates. As for their consciences—but I will say no more, for fear I have already said too much.

**ENQUIRER:** Your position does not seem very enviable to me.

**THEOSOPHIST:** It is not. But don't you think there must be—

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...[must surely recognize] something very noble, very exalted, and very true behind the Society and its philosophy, when the leaders and the founders of the movement still continue to work for it with all their strength? They sacrifice all comfort, all worldly prosperity, and success to it—even their good names and reputations—yes, even their honor. In return, they receive incessant and ceaseless public abuse <note>original: "obloquy"; meaning strong public condemnation or verbal abuse.</note>, relentless persecution, untiring slander, and constant ingratitude. Their best efforts are misunderstood, and they deal with blows and attacks from all sides. Yet, by simply dropping their work, they would find themselves immediately released from every responsibility and shielded from every further attack.

**ENQUIRER:** I confess, such perseverance seems very astounding to me, and I have wondered why you did all this.

**THEOSOPHIST:** Believe me, it is for no self-gratification. It is only in the hope of training a few individuals to carry on our work for humanity according to its original program when the Founders are dead and gone. They have already found a few such noble and devoted souls to replace them. Thanks to these few, coming generations will find the path to peace a little less thorny and the way a little widened. Thus, all this suffering will have produced good results, and their self-sacrifice will not have been in vain. At present, the main, fundamental object of the Society is to sow seeds <note>original: "germs"; used here in the sense of the earliest stage of an idea or development.</note> in the hearts of men, which may in time sprout and, under more favorable <note>original: "propitious"</note> circumstances, lead to a healthy reform—one that brings more happiness to the masses than they have enjoyed until now.

## XIII.

# ON THE MISCONCEPTIONS ABOUT THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY.

---

### *THEOSOPHY AND ASCETICISM.*

**ENQUIRER:** I have heard people say that your rules require all members to be vegetarians, celibates <note>People who abstain from marriage and sexual relations, usually for religious reasons.</note>, and rigid ascetics <note>Those who practice severe self-discipline and avoid all forms of indulgence for spiritual goals.</note>; but you have not told me anything of the sort yet. Can you tell me the truth once and for all about this?

**THEOSOPHIST:** The truth is that our rules require nothing of the kind. The Theosophical Society does not even expect, far less require, *any* of its members to be ascetics in any way—unless you call it "asceticism" to try and benefit other people and be unselfish in your own life.

**ENQUIRER:** But many of your members are still strict vegetarians and openly declare <note>original: "avow"</note> their intention to remain unmarried. This is also most often the case with those who take a leading role in the work of your Society.

**THEOSOPHIST:** That is only natural, because most of our truly dedicated workers are members of the Inner Section <note>A more private group within the Society dedicated to deeper spiritual study and stricter personal discipline.</note> of the Society, which I told you about before.

<vocab>Theosophical Society, Theosophy, asceticism, vegetarians, celibates, Inner Section, unselfishness</vocab>

<summary>This page clarifies that the Theosophical Society does not require its general members to practice asceticism, vegetarianism, or celibacy, noting that such disciplines are usually voluntary choices made by dedicated workers in the Society's "Inner Section."</summary>

<keywords>Theosophy, asceticism, vegetarianism, celibacy, Inner Section, membership requirements, misconceptions, unselfishness</keywords>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQUIRER:** Oh! Then do you require ascetic practices in that Inner Section?

**THEOSOPHIST:** No; we do not require or demand <note>original: "enjoin"</note> them even there. However, I see that I had better give you an explanation of our views on the subject of asceticism in general; then you will understand our position on vegetarianism and so on.

**ENQUIRER:** Please proceed.

**THEOSOPHIST:** As I have already told you, most people who become truly earnest students of <term>Theosophy</term> <note>A system of esoteric philosophy seeking direct knowledge of the divine and the underlying reality of nature.</note> and active workers in our Society wish to do more than just theoretically study the truths we teach. They wish to *know* the truth through their own direct personal experience. They want to study <term>Occultism</term> <note>The study of hidden spiritual laws and the powers latent in humanity.</note> with the goal of acquiring the wisdom and power they feel they need to help others effectively and wisely, instead of blindly and randomly. Therefore, sooner or later, they join the <term>Inner Section</term> <note>A private group within the Society dedicated to deeper spiritual practice and the study of esoteric teachings.</note>.

**ENQUIRER:** But you said that "ascetic practices" are not mandatory even in that Inner Section?

**THEOSOPHIST:** They are not; but the first thing members learn there is a true understanding <note>original: "conception"</note> of the relationship between the body—the physical shell—and the inner, true man. The relationship and mutual interaction between these two aspects of human nature are explained and demonstrated to them. Consequently, they soon become convinced of the supreme importance of the inner man over the outer case or body. They are taught that blind, unintelligent asceticism is mere folly. Conduct like that of Saint Labre <note>original: "St. Labro." Referring to Saint Benedict Joseph Labre (1748–1783), a Catholic saint known for his extreme poverty and neglect of physical hygiene.</note>—whom I mentioned before—or that of the Indian <term>Fakirs</term> <note>Ascetics or religious mendicants who often perform feats of endurance or self-mortification.</note> and jungle ascetics who cut, burn, and waste away <note>original: "macerate"</note> their bodies in the most cruel and horrible manner, is simply self-

<summary>The Theosophist explains that while the Inner Section does not mandate asceticism, it teaches students the priority of the spiritual "inner man" over the physical body, dismissing extreme physical self-torture as foolishness.</summary>
<keywords>Inner Section, Occultism, Theosophy, asceticism, Saint Labre, Fakirs, physical body vs. inner man</keywords>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...torture for selfish ends—such as merely to develop will-power—but is perfectly useless for the purpose of assisting true spiritual, or Theosophical, development.

**ENQUIRER:** I see; you consider only *moral* self-discipline <note>original: "asceticism"</note> to be necessary. It serves as a means to an end—that end being the perfect balance <note>original: "equilibrium"</note> of the *inner* nature of a human being and the attainment of complete mastery over the body, along with all its passions and desires?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Exactly. But these methods must be used intelligently and wisely, not blindly and foolishly. It is like an athlete who is training and preparing for a great competition, rather than like a miser who starves himself into illness just to satisfy his obsession <note>original: "passion"</note> for gold.

**ENQUIRER:** I understand your general idea now; but let us see how you apply it in practice. What about vegetarianism, for instance?

**THEOSOPHIST:** One of the great German scientists has shown that every kind of animal tissue, no matter how it is cooked, still retains certain distinct characteristics of the animal to which it belonged—characteristics that can be recognized. Aside from that, everyone knows by the taste what kind of meat they are eating. We go a step further and prove that when animal flesh is absorbed <note>original: "assimilated"</note> by a human as food, it physically imparts some of the traits <note>original: "characteristics"</note> of that animal to them. Moreover, <term>occult science</term> <note>The study of hidden spiritual laws and the powers latent in humanity.</note> teaches and proves this to its students through direct observation <note>original: "ocular demonstration"</note>. It also shows that this "coarsening" or "animalizing" effect on humans is strongest from the meat of larger animals, less from birds, even less from fish and other cold-blooded animals, and least of all when one eats only vegetables.

**ENQUIRER:** Then would it be better for a person not to eat at all?

<summary>The author explains that self-discipline should be a tool for spiritual balance rather than a selfish exercise of will. The text also argues for vegetarianism by claiming that consuming animal flesh physically transfers animalistic traits to humans.</summary>

**THEOSOPHIST:** If he could live without eating, of course it would. But as things stand, he must eat to live, and so we advise truly dedicated <note>original: "earnest"</note> students to eat food that will least clog and weigh down their brains and bodies. We suggest food that will have the smallest effect in hindering and slowing the development of their intuition, as well as their inner faculties and powers.

**ENQUIRER:** Then you do not adopt all the arguments that vegetarians usually use?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Certainly not. Some of their arguments are very weak and are often based on entirely false assumptions. But on the other hand, many of the things they say are quite true. For instance, we believe that much disease—and especially the strong tendency <note>original: "predisposition"</note> toward illness that is becoming such a prominent feature of our time—is largely due to eating meat, especially canned <note>original: "tinned"</note> meats. But it would take too long to thoroughly examine the merits of vegetarianism right now, so please move on to another topic.

**ENQUIRER:** One more question. What should members of your <term>Inner Section</term> <note>The Esoteric Section of the Theosophical Society, consisting of students committed to deeper spiritual practice and stricter discipline.</note> do regarding their diet when they are ill?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Follow the best practical advice they can get, of course. Do you not yet realize <note>original: "grasp"</note> that we never impose any rigid <note>original: "hard-and-fast"</note> obligations in this regard? Remember once and for all that in all such matters, we take a rational, and never a fanatical, view of things. If, due to illness or long-standing habit, a person cannot go without meat, then by all means, let them eat it. It is no crime; it will only slightly delay <note>original: "retard"</note> their progress. After all is said and done, purely physical actions and bodily functions are far less important than what a person thinks and *feels*, what

<summary>The author explains that while Theosophy recommends a vegetarian diet to aid spiritual intuition and physical health, it rejects fanatical rules, prioritizing a person's inner thoughts and feelings over rigid dietary restrictions, especially during illness.</summary>

**THEOSOPHIST:** They are even worse for one's moral and spiritual growth than eating meat. Alcohol in all its forms has a direct, significant, and very harmful <note>original: "deleterious"</note> influence on a person's mental and spiritual state <note>original: "psychic condition"</note>. Drinking wine and spirits is only slightly less destructive to the development of inner powers than the habitual use of hashish, opium, and similar drugs.

---

### *THEOSOPHY AND MARRIAGE*

**ENQUIRER:** Now to another question: must a person marry or remain single <note>original: "a celibate"</note>?

**THEOSOPHIST:** That depends on the type of person you mean. Are you referring to someone who intends to live in the world? This might be someone who—even if they are a good, dedicated <note>original: "earnest"</note> Theosophist and a passionate worker for our cause—still has ties and desires that bind them to the world. In short, they do not feel they are finished forever with what people call "life," and they do not yet desire one thing and one thing only—to know the truth and to be able to help others. For such a person, I say there is no reason why they should not marry, if they want to take the risks of that lottery where there are so many more losing tickets <note>original: "blanks"</note> than prizes. Surely you cannot believe us so absurd and fanatical as to preach against marriage altogether? On the contrary, except in a few exceptional cases involving the practice of <term>Occultism</term> <note>In Theosophy, this refers to the scientific study of the hidden laws of nature and the disciplined development of one's latent spiritual faculties.</note>, marriage is the only remedy against immorality.

**ENQUIRER:** But why can't a person acquire this knowledge and power while living a married life?

**THEOSOPHIST:** My dear sir, I cannot go into physiological questions with you; however, I can give you an obvious and, I think, sufficient answer that will explain the moral reasons for our stance. Can a person serve two masters? No! In the same way, it is impossible for someone to divide their attention between the pursuit of <term>Occultism</term> <note>The study and practice of hidden spiritual laws and the development of inner powers.</note> and a spouse. If they try, they will certainly fail to do either properly. Let me remind you: practical Occultism is far too serious and dangerous a study for anyone to undertake unless they are completely serious <note>original: "deadly earnest"</note> and ready to sacrifice *everything*—themselves first of all—to achieve their goal. But this does not apply to the members of our Inner Section <note>The "Inner Section" refers to the Esoteric Section of the Theosophical Society, intended for those seeking deeper spiritual training.</note>. I am only referring to those who are determined to walk the path of discipleship that leads to the highest goal. Most, if not all, of those who join our Inner Section are only beginners, preparing themselves in this life to truly enter that path in future lives.

---

### *THEOSOPHY AND EDUCATION*

**ENQUIRER:** One of your strongest arguments for the inadequacy of current Western religions—and to some extent the materialistic philosophy that is so popular now, but which you seem to view as a "devastating corruption" <note>original: "abomination of desolation," a biblical phrase meaning a horrific desecration or spiritual ruin</note>—is the vast amount of misery and suffering that undeniably exists, especially in our large cities. But surely you must recognize how much has been and is being done to fix this situation through the spread of education and the sharing of knowledge <note>original: "diffusion of intelligence"</note>.

**THEOSOPHIST:** Future generations will hardly thank you for such a "spread of knowledge," nor will your current education system do much good for the poor, starving masses.

**ENQUIRER:** Ah! But you must give us time. It has only been a few years since we began to educate the general public.

**THEOSOPHIST:** And what, I ask you, has your Christian religion been doing since the 1400s <note>original: "fifteenth century"</note>, once you acknowledge that the education of the masses has not even been attempted until now? This is the very work—if there ever was one—which a Christian (that is <note>original: "i.e."</note>, a Christ-following) church and people ought to perform.

**ENQUIRER:** Well, you may be right; but now—

**THEOSOPHIST:** Just let us consider this question of education from a broad standpoint, and I will prove to you that you are doing more harm than good with many of your boasted improvements. The schools for the poorer children, though far less useful than they ought to be, are good in contrast with the vile surroundings to which they are doomed by your modern society. However, the infusion of a little practical <term>Theosophy</term> <note>A philosophy teaching that all religions contain a kernel of the same spiritual truth and emphasizing universal brotherhood.</note> would help the poor, suffering masses a hundred times more in life than all this infusion of useless information <note>original: "intelligence"</note>.

**THEOSOPHIST:** Let me finish, please. You have opened a subject on which we Theosophists feel deeply, and I must have my say. Everything is orderly; there, the child learns to sing and to play; they have toys that awaken their intelligence; they learn to use their fingers skillfully; they are spoken to with a smile instead of a frown; they are gently corrected or encouraged instead of cursed. All this humanizes the children, stimulates their minds, and makes them receptive to intellectual and moral influences. The schools are not everything they could or should be; but, compared to the children's homes, they are paradises, and they are slowly influencing those homes for the better. In this way, they are trained for a life of ferocious selfish—

selfishness and the struggle for honors and rewards <note>original: "emoluments"</note> instead of kindly feelings toward others.

**THEOSOPHIST:** And what are these examinations—the terror of modern boyhood and youth? They are simply a method of classification used to record the results of your school teaching in tables. In other words, they represent the practical application of modern scientific methods to the *human species* <note>original: *genus homo*</note> *in terms of their intellect* <note>original: *qua intellection*</note>. Now, "science" teaches that intellect is merely a result of the mechanical interaction of brain matter <note>original: "brain-stuff"</note>; therefore, it is only logical that modern education should be almost entirely mechanical—a sort of automatic machine for manufacturing intellect by the ton. Very little experience with examinations is needed to show that the education they produce is simply a training of the physical memory; sooner or later, all your schools will sink to this level. As for any real, sound cultivation of thinking and reasoning powers, it is simply impossible as long as everything must be judged by the results of competitive examinations. Furthermore, school training is of the highest importance in forming character, especially regarding its moral direction. Now, from start to finish, your modern system is based on so-called scientific revelations: "the struggle for existence" and the "survival of the fittest." Throughout his early life, every man has these ideas driven into him by practical example and experience, as well as by direct teaching, until it is impossible to remove from his mind the idea that the "self"—the lower, personal, animal self—is the ultimate purpose and goal of life. This is where you find the great source of all the subsequent—

misery, crime, and heartless selfishness, which you admit just as much as I do. Selfishness, as said over and over again, is the curse of humanity and the prolific parent of all the evils and crimes in this life; and it is your schools which are the hotbeds of such selfishness.

**ENQUIRER:** That sounds fine as a general statement, but I should like a few facts and to learn also how this can be remedied.

**THEOSOPHIST:** Very well, I will try and satisfy you. There are three great divisions of educational institutions <note>original: "scholastic establishments"</note>: board, middle-class, and public schools, running up the scale from the most grossly commercial to the idealistic classical, with many variations and combinations. The practical, commercial approach gives rise to the "modern side" <note>a curriculum focusing on modern languages and sciences rather than Latin and Greek</note>, and the traditional and orthodox classical curriculum reflects its heavy respectability even as far as the training colleges for elementary school teachers <note>original: "School Board pupil teacher's establishments"</note>. Here we plainly see scientific and material commerce replacing the worn-out <note>original: "effete"</note> orthodox and classical systems. The reason is not very hard to find. The objects of this branch of education are, then, pounds, shillings, and pence—the **highest good** <note>original: *summum bonum*</note> of the 19th century. Thus, the energies generated by the brain molecules of its followers are all concentrated on one point. They are, therefore, to some extent, an organized army of educated and speculative intellects representing a minority of men, trained to oppose the masses of ignorant, simple-minded people who are doomed to be preyed upon <note>original: "vampirised"</note>, lived off of, and oppressed by their intellectually stronger brothers. Such training is not only untheosophical, it is simply **UNCHRISTIAN**. Result: The direct outcome of this branch of education is an overflooding of the market with money-

<summary>The Theosophist argues that the modern education system is a "hotbed of selfishness" that prioritizes financial gain over morality, creating an intellectual elite that exploits the uneducated masses.</summary>

...making machines, with heartless, selfish men—animals—who have been most carefully trained to prey on their fellows and take advantage of the ignorance of their weaker brothers!

**ENQUIRER:** Well, but you cannot assert that of our great public schools <note>In the UK, "public schools" refers to elite, private fee-paying institutions like Eton or Harrow</note>, at any rate?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Not exactly, it is true. But though the *form* is different, the animating spirit is the same: *un-theosophical and un-Christian*, whether Eton and Harrow turn out scientists or clergymen and theologians.

**ENQUIRER:** Surely you don't mean to call Eton and Harrow "commercial"?

**THEOSOPHIST:** No. Of course the classical system <note>an education focused on Latin, Greek, and traditional humanities</note> is above all things respectable, and in the present day is productive of some good. It still remains the favorite at our great public schools, where not only an intellectual but also a social education is obtainable. It is, therefore, of prime importance that the less-gifted <note>original: "dull"</note> boys of aristocratic and wealthy parents should go to such schools to meet the rest of the young life of the "noble" <note>original: "blood"</note> and moneyed classes. But unfortunately, there is a huge competition even for entrance; for the wealthy classes are increasing, and poor but clever boys seek to enter the public schools through the lucrative scholarships, both at the schools themselves and from them to the Universities.

**ENQUIRER:** According to this view, the wealthier "less-gifted students" <note>original: "dullards"</note> have to work even harder than their poorer classmates?

**THEOSOPHIST:** It is so. But, strange to say, the faithful followers of the cult of the "survival of the fittest" <note>the theory that only the best-adapted individuals survive, a concept from Charles Darwin and Herbert Spencer often applied to social competition</note> do not practice their own creed; for their whole effort is to make the naturally unfit replace the fit. Thus, by bribes of large sums of money, they attract the best...

<summary>The author critiques the elite British public school system, arguing that it prioritizes social networking for the wealthy over genuine merit. <page-num>269</page-num>

...teachers from their natural pupils to turn their naturally unsuitable children into machines <note>original: "mechanicalise"</note> for professions that they overcrowd to no purpose.

**ENQUIRER:** And to what do you attribute all this?

**THEOSOPHIST:** All this is due to the harmfulness of a system that turns out "goods to order," regardless of the natural inclinations and talents of the youth. The poor little candidate for this "progressive paradise" of learning comes almost straight from the nursery to the treadmill of a preparatory school for "sons of gentlemen." Here he is immediately seized by the workers of the material-intellectual factory and crammed with Latin, French, and Greek grammar <note>original: "accidence"; the basic rules for how words change form, such as conjugations and declensions</note>, dates, and tables. If he has any natural genius, it is rapidly squeezed out of him by the rollers of what Carlyle <note>Thomas Carlyle (1795–1881) was a famous Scottish philosopher and social critic who often criticized the soul-crushing nature of modern industry and education</note> so aptly called "dead words" <note>original: "vocables"; words studied for their sound or form without regard for their living meaning or spirit</note>.

**ENQUIRER:** But surely he is taught something besides "dead words," and much of that might lead him directly toward *Theosophy*, if not entirely into the Theosophical Society?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Not much. For in history, he will only gain enough knowledge of his own particular nation to equip him with a steel armor of prejudice against all other peoples. He will be soaked in the foul cesspools of recorded national hate and bloodthirstiness; surely, you would not call that—*Theosophy*?

**ENQUIRER:** What are your further objections?

**THEOSOPHIST:** Added to this is a mere smattering of so-called "Biblical facts," from the study of which all intellect is removed. It is simply a memory lesson; the "Why" provided by the teacher is a "Why" based on circumstances and not on reason.

**ENQUIRER:** Yes; but I have heard you congratulate yourself on the ever-increasing number of Agnostics and Atheists in our day, so it appears that even people trained in the system you criticize so heartily *do* learn to think and reason for themselves.

**THEOSOPHIST:** Yes; but it is more because of a healthy reaction against *that* system than because of the system itself. In our Society, we infinitely prefer Agnostics, and even outright Atheists, to bigots of any religion. An Agnostic’s mind is always open to the truth, whereas bigotry blinds the bigot just as the sun blinds an owl. The best—that is <note>original: "i.e." (Latin: id est)</note>, the most truth-loving, philanthropic, and honest—of our Fellows were, and are, Agnostics and Atheists (disbelievers in a *personal* God <note>A "personal God" refers to the concept of a deity with human-like attributes and personality, as opposed to the abstract, universal principle favored by Theosophists</note>). But there are no free-thinking boys and girls, and generally early training will leave its mark behind in the form of a cramped and distorted mind. A proper and sane system of education should produce the most vigorous and open minds, strictly trained in logical and accurate thought, and not in blind faith. How can you ever expect good results while you corrupt the reasoning abilities of your children by telling them to believe in the miracles of the Bible on Sunday, while for the six other days of the week you teach them that such things are scientifically impossible?

**ENQUIRER:** What would you prefer, then?

**THEOSOPHIST:** If we had money, we would establish schools which would produce something more than just people who can read and write but are destined for poverty <note>original: "candidates for starvation"</note>. Children should, above all, be taught self-reliance, love for all people, altruism, mutual charity, and more than anything else, to think and reason for themselves. We would reduce...

...the purely mechanical work of rote memorization to an absolute minimum, and instead devote time to the development and training of the inner senses, mental faculties, and hidden potential. We would endeavor to treat each child as an individual and educate them in a way that produces the most harmonious and equal unfolding of their powers, so that their specific talents can find their full natural development. Our goal would be to create *free men and women*—intellectually free, morally free, completely unprejudiced, and above all else, *unselfish*. We believe that much, if not all, of this could be achieved through a proper and truly theosophical education.

### WHY, THEN, IS THERE SO MUCH PREJUDICE AGAINST THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY? <note>original: "T.S."</note>

**ENQUIRER:** If Theosophy is even half of what you say, why is there such terrible ill-will toward it? This is even more of a puzzle than anything else.

**THEOSOPHIST:** It is; but you must keep in mind how many powerful enemies we have provoked since our Society was formed. As I just said, if the Theosophical movement were simply one of those many modern fads—as harmless in the end as they are fleeting <note>original: "evanescent"</note>—it would simply be laughed at and left alone, as it currently is by those who still do not understand its real purpose. But it is nothing of the kind. At its core, Theosophy is the most serious movement of this era. Moreover, it is one that threatens the very existence of most of the long-standing frauds <note>original: "humbugs"</note>, prejudices, and social evils of our time—those evils that enrich and satisfy the upper...

Yet, while they are invisible to most of the members of our Society, they are well known to the true "Founders" and the protectors of our Society. **THEOSOPHIST:** I never said *I* knew them personally. I may or may not know them—but I know *of* them, and this is enough; and I defy them to do their worst. They may achieve great harm <note>original: "mischief"</note> and throw our ranks into confusion, especially among the faint-hearted...

**ENQUIRER:** Can you give me more details about these, so that I may know what to answer when asked? Give me a brief history of the Society, in short, and explain why the world believes all this.

Very soon, the Society came to be regarded as a group <note>original: "body"</note> pretending to possess "miraculous" powers. The world never realized...

This hatred was expressed through every kind of slander, unkind personal remarks, and absurd misrepresentations of Theosophical teachings in all the American Spiritualist publications <note>original: "organs"</note>. For years we were persecuted, denounced, and...

Since Theosophy does not agree with any single sect or creed, it is considered the enemy of all alike, because it teaches that they are all, more or less, mistaken. The missionaries in India hated and tried to crush us because they saw the best and brightest <note>original: "the flower"</note> of the educated Indian youth and the Brahmins—who are almost impossible for missionaries to reach—joining the Society in large numbers. And yet, apart from this general class hatred, the T.S. counts many clergymen, and even one or two bishops, among its ranks.

**Enquirer:** And what led the Society for Psychical Research <note>original: "S.P.R."</note> to take a stand against you? You were both pursuing the same line of study in some respects, and several of the Psychic Researchers belonged to your society.

**Theosophist:** At first, we were very good friends with the leaders of the S.P.R. However, when the attack on the phenomena appeared in the *Christian College Magazine* <note>Referring to the 1884 "Coulomb Affair," where a former servant at the Theosophical headquarters accused Blavatsky of fraud.</note>—supported by the alleged revelations of a servant <note>original: "menial"</note>—the S.P.R. found that they had compromised themselves by publishing too many...

...many of the phenomena that had occurred in connection with the Theosophical Society <note>original: "T.S."</note>. Their ambition is to pose as an authoritative and strictly scientific body. Consequently, they had to choose between maintaining that position by abandoning the Society—and even trying to destroy it—or being grouped, in the opinion of the materialistic skeptics <note>original: "Sadducees," a reference to the biblical sect that denied the existence of spirits or the resurrection.</note> of high society <note>original: "grand monde"</note>, with "credulous" Theosophists and Spiritualists. But they were so desperate to find a seemingly reasonable motive for the life of devotion and ceaseless labor led by the two Founders <note>Helena Blavatsky and Henry Steel Olcott.</note>, and for the complete lack of any financial <note>original: "pecuniary"</note> profit or other advantage to them, that our enemies were forced to resort to the utterly absurd, eminently ridiculous, and now famous "Russian spy theory" to explain this devotion. But the old saying, "The blood of the martyrs is the seed of the Church," proved correct once more. After the initial shock of this attack, the Theosophical Society doubled and tripled its numbers, but the negative impression created still remains. A French author was right in saying, "Slander, slander always and again; some of it will always stick" <note>original: "Calomniez, calomniez toujours et encore, il en restera toujours quelque chose."</note>. This is why unjust prejudices are common today, and why everything connected with the Society, and especially its Founders, is so falsely distorted, because it is based on malicious hearsay alone.

**ENQUIRER:** Yet in the fourteen years the Society has existed, surely you have had ample time and opportunity to show yourselves and your work in their true light?

**THEOSOPHIST:** How, or when, have we ever been given such an opportunity?

--- Page 295 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the public perception of the Theosophical Society and the difficulty the organization faced in defending its reputation against rumors and slander.</meta>

<page-num>277</page-num>

-> # THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. <-

Our most prominent members had an aversion to anything that seemed like publicly justifying themselves. Their policy has always been: “We must live it down,” and “What does it matter what the newspapers say or what people think?” The Society was too poor to send out public lecturers. Therefore, the explanations <note>original: "expositions"</note> of our views and doctrines were limited to a few Theosophical books that were successful, but which people often misunderstood or only knew about through hearsay. Our journals were, and still are, boycotted; our literary works are ignored. To this day, no one seems even to feel quite certain whether Theosophists are a kind of serpent and devil worshippers, or simply “Esoteric Buddhists”—whatever that may mean. 

It was useless for us to go on denying, day after day and year after year, every kind of unbelievable tall tale <note>original: "cock-and-bull stories"</note> about us; for, as soon as one was dealt with, another—even more absurd and malicious—was born from the ashes of the first. Unfortunately, human nature is such that any good thing said of a person is immediately forgotten and never repeated. But one has only to utter a slander <note>original: "calumny"</note> or to start a story—no matter how absurd, false, or incredible it may be, as long as it is connected with some unpopular person—for it to be successful and immediately accepted as a historical fact. Like *Don Basilio’s* “SLANDER” <note>original: "CALUMNIA," a reference to an aria in Rossini's opera *The Barber of Seville* where the character Don Basilio describes how a rumor grows into a storm.</note>, the rumor springs up at first as a soft, gentle breeze hardly stirring the grass under your feet, arising from no one knows where; then, in a very short time, it is transformed into a strong wind, begins to blow a gale, and immediately becomes a roaring storm!

--- Page 296 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion regarding the pervasive nature of slander and rumors directed at the Theosophical Society, following the previous page's metaphor of how a small rumor grows into a destructive storm.</meta>

<page-num>278</page-num>

-> # THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. <-

Slander <note>original: "calumny"</note> among the news is like what an octopus is among fish; it sucks into one’s mind and fastens onto our memory, which feeds upon it, leaving permanent <note>original: "indelible"</note> marks even after the slander itself has been physically destroyed. A slanderous lie is the only master-key that will open any and every brain. It is sure to be welcomed and hosted in every human mind, from the highest to the lowest, if there is even a little prejudice—no matter how lowly the source or how foul the motive behind it.

**ENQ.** Don’t you think your claim is a bit too broad? The English have never been too quick to believe everything they hear, and our nation is famous <note>original: "proverbially known"</note> for its love of fair play. A lie has no legs to stand on for long, and—

**THEO.** An Englishman is just as ready to believe evil as a person of any other nation; for it is human nature, not a national characteristic. As for lies, if they have no legs to stand on, as the proverb says, they certainly have very fast wings. They can and do fly farther and wider than any other kind of news, in England as elsewhere. Remember, lies and slander are the only kind of literature we can always get for free <note>original: "gratis"</note> without paying a subscription. We can try an experiment if you like. Since you are so interested in Theosophical matters and have heard so much about us, why don't you ask me about as many of these rumors and "hearsays" as you can think of? I will give you the truth and nothing but the truth, and it will be open to the strictest verification.

**ENQ.** Before we change the subject, let us have the whole truth on this point. Some writers have called your teachings “immoral and harmful” <note>original: "pernicious"</note>; others, because many so-called “authorities”...

<page-num>279</page-num>

<header>-> THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. <-</header>

...and scholars of the East <note>original: "Orientalists"</note> find nothing but sex-worship in its many forms within Indian religions, they accuse you of teaching nothing better than phallic <note>Phallicism refers to the worship of the reproductive organs as symbols of nature's creative powers.</note> worship. They claim that since modern Theosophy is so closely linked with Eastern—and particularly Indian—thought, it must be corrupted by this same taint. Occasionally, they even go so far as to accuse European Theosophists of reviving the practices associated with this cult. What do you say to this?

**THEO.** I have heard and read this before, and my response is that no lie or slander <note>original: "calumny"</note> has ever been invented and spread that is more completely without foundation. "Silly people see nothing but silly dreams," says a Russian proverb. It makes one's blood boil to hear such disgusting accusations made without the slightest evidence, based only on mere assumptions. Ask the hundreds of respectable English men and women who have been members of the Theosophical Society for years whether they were ever taught an immoral rule or a *harmful* <note>original: "pernicious"</note> doctrine. Open *The Secret Doctrine* <note>H.P. Blavatsky’s primary foundational work, published in 1888, which outlines her system of thought.</note>, and you will find page after page condemning the Jews and other nations specifically because of this devotion to phallic rituals. These rituals resulted from a literal <note>original: "dead letter"</note> interpretation of nature symbolism and the crudely physical <note>original: "grossly materialistic"</note> understanding of nature's duality found in all public <note>original: "exoteric"</note> religions. Such constant and malicious misrepresentation of our teachings and beliefs is truly disgraceful.

**ENQ.** But you cannot deny that the phallic element *does* exist in the religions of the East?

**THEO.** I do not deny it; however, I maintain that this proves no more than its presence in Christianity, the religion of the West. Read Hargrave Jennings' *The Rosicrucians* <note>Hargrave Jennings (1817–1890) was a British author who wrote extensively on phallicism and the occult, often arguing that religious symbols had sexual origins.</note> if you want to...

<summary>The Theosophist vehemently denies accusations that Theosophy involves "phallic worship," arguing that while such symbols exist in the literal interpretations of Eastern and Western religions, Theosophical doctrine explicitly condemns these materialistic views.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophy, phallic worship, Indian religions, The Secret Doctrine, symbolism, sexual rituals, Hargrave Jennings, religious slander, exoteric vs. esoteric</keywords>

--- Page 298 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue where the Theosophist (THEO.) is refuting accusations that Theosophy is linked to "phallic worship" or immoral Eastern sects.</meta>

<page-num>280</page-num>

<header>-> THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY. <-</header>

Rest assured of it. In the East, phallic symbolism <note>Phallic symbolism refers to the religious use of symbols representing the creative forces of nature, which critics often misinterpreted as purely sexual.</note> is perhaps more straightforward because it is more faithful to nature—or, I would rather say, more innocent and honest than in the West. But it is not more sexually unrestrained <note>original: "licentious"</note>, nor does it suggest the same vulgar and coarse ideas to the Eastern mind as it does to the Western mind. There are, perhaps, one or two exceptions, such as the shameful sect known as the "Great King" <note>original: "Maharajah"</note> or the followers of the teacher Vallabha <note>original: "Vallabhachârya"; this was a controversial Hindu sect that faced a famous 1862 libel case regarding the conduct of its leaders.</note>.

**ENQUIRER.** A writer in the *Agnostic Journal*—one of your accusers—has just hinted that the followers of this disgraceful sect are Theosophists and "claim true Theosophic insight."

**THEOSOPHIST.** He wrote a falsehood, and that is all there is to it. There never was, nor is there at present, one single follower of Vallabha in our Society. As to their having or claiming "Theosophic insight," that is another untruth <note>original: "fib"</note> based on total ignorance of Indian sects. Their "Great King" <note>original: "Maharajah"</note> only claims a right to the money, wives, and daughters of his foolish followers, and nothing more. This sect is despised by all other Hindus.

But you will find the whole subject dealt with at length in *The Secret Doctrine* <note>H.P. Blavatsky’s primary work, published in 1888, which outlines the history and philosophy of Theosophy.</note>, to which I must again refer you for detailed explanations. To conclude: the very soul of Theosophy is completely opposed to phallic worship. Its occult <note>original: "occult or esoteric"; refers to the private, inner circle of students studying hidden spiritual laws.</note> section is even more opposed to it than its public <note>original: "exoteric"</note> teachings. There never was a more dishonest statement made than the one you mentioned. And now, ask me some other questions.

<summary>In this passage, the author strongly denies any connection between Theosophy and "phallic worship," specifically distancing the movement from the controversial Vallabhacharya sect. The text emphasizes that Theosophical teachings, both public and private, are fundamentally opposed to such practices.</summary>

<header>THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.</header>

### *IS THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY A MONEY-MAKING CONCERN?*

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> Agreed. Well, have either of the Founders—Colonel H. S. Olcott or H. P. Blavatsky—ever made any money or profit, or derived any worldly benefit from the <term>Theosophical Society</term>, as some newspapers claim?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> Not one penny. The newspapers lie. On the contrary, they have both given everything they had and have literally impoverished <note>original: "beggared"</note> themselves. As for “worldly benefits,” think of the slanders <note>original: "calumnies"</note> and vilification they have been subjected to, and then ask that question!

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> Yet I have read in many missionary publications <note>original: "organs"</note> that the entrance fees and subscriptions more than covered all expenses; one even said that the Founders were making twenty thousand pounds a year!

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> This is a lie <note>original: "fib"</note>, like many others. In the published accounts of January 1889, you will find an exact statement of *all* the money ever received from any source since 1879. The total received from all sources—entrance fees, donations, and so on—during these ten years is under six thousand pounds <note>Approximately £800,000 in modern value, but spread over a decade for an international organization.</note>.

A large part of this sum was contributed by the Founders themselves from their private resources and the proceeds of their literary work. All of this has been openly and officially admitted, even by our enemies, the <term>Society for Psychical Research</term> <note>original: "Psychic Research Society"; this refers to the organization that famously investigated Blavatsky's claims in 1884.</note>. 

Now both Founders are penniless. One <note>H. P. Blavatsky</note> is too old and ill to work as she did before; unable to spare time for outside literary work to help the Society financially, she can only write for the Theosophical cause. The other <note>H. S. Olcott</note> continues to labor for it as before, and receives just as little thanks for it.

<summary>The Theosophist refutes claims that the Society's founders profited financially, asserting that they actually spent their own money to support the cause and are now penniless.</summary>
<keywords>H. S. Olcott, H. P. Blavatsky, Theosophical Society finances, Society for Psychical Research, missionary publications, financial transparency</keywords>

--- Page 300 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the financial integrity of the Theosophical Society's founders, H.P. Blavatsky and Colonel Olcott.</meta>

<page-num>282</page-num>

<header>THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.</header>

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> But surely they need money to live?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> Not at all. As long as they have food and lodging—even if they owe these to the devotion of a few friends—they need little more.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> But couldn't Madame Blavatsky, especially, earn more than enough to live on through her writings?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> When she was in India, she received an average of several thousand rupees <note>original: "some thousand rupees"; the currency of India. At the time, this was a significant sum for an individual but modest for supporting an international movement.</note> a year for articles contributed to Russian and other newspapers, but she gave it all away to the Society.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> Were they political articles?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> Never. Everything she wrote throughout her seven-year stay in India is currently in print. It deals only with the religions, ethnology <note>The study of the characteristics of various peoples and the differences and relationships between them.</note>, and customs of India, and with Theosophy—never with politics, which she knows nothing about and cares for even less. Furthermore, two years ago she refused several contracts totaling about 1,200 gold rubles <note>original: "roubles in gold"; the currency of the Russian Empire.</note> per month. She could not accept them without abandoning her work for the Society, which required all her time and energy. She has documents to prove this.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> But why couldn't both she and Colonel Olcott do what others—notably many Theosophists—do: follow their respective professions and devote their spare <note>original: "surplus"</note> time to the Society's work?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> Because by "serving two masters," either the professional or the philanthropic work would have suffered. Every true Theosophist is morally bound to sacrifice personal interests for impersonal ones—their own *present* well-being for the *future* benefit of others. If the Founders do not set the example, who will?

<summary>The Theosophist explains that the founders live modestly and have sacrificed significant personal income from writing and professional careers to focus entirely on the Society's mission. They argue that leadership requires prioritizing the collective good over personal gain to set a moral example for other members.</summary>

<keywords>Madame Blavatsky, Colonel Olcott, financial sacrifice, India, philanthropy, gold rubles, rupees, Theosophical Society, altruism</keywords>

--- Page 301 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the financial status of the Theosophical Society and the personal finances of Madame Blavatsky, refuting rumors of large private donations.</meta>

<page-num>283</page-num>

<header>THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.</header>

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> And are there many who follow it?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> I am bound to tell you the truth. In Europe, there are about half a dozen in all, out of more than that number of Branches.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> Then it is not true that the Theosophical Society has a large capital or endowment <note>A permanent fund or source of income bestowed upon an institution.</note> of its own?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> It is false, for it has none at all. Now that the entrance fee of £1 and the small annual dues have been abolished, it is even doubtful whether the staff at the headquarters in India will not soon starve to death.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> Then why not raise subscriptions?

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> We are not the Salvation Army; we cannot—and *have never*—begged. Nor have we ever followed the example of the churches and sects by "taking up collections." The money that is occasionally sent for the support of the Society, and the small sums contributed by some devoted Fellows <note>original: "Fellows"; members of the Theosophical Society.</note>, are all voluntary donations.

<term>ENQUIRER.</term> But I have heard of large sums of money given to Madame Blavatsky. It was said four years ago that she received £5,000 from one rich, young "Fellow" who went to join them in India, and £10,000 from another wealthy and well-known American gentleman—one of your members who died in Europe four years ago.

<term>THEOSOPHIST.</term> Tell those who told you this that they are either lying themselves or repeating a gross falsehood. Madame Blavatsky has *never* asked for or received ONE PENNY from the two gentlemen mentioned above, nor anything like that amount from anyone else, since the Theosophical Society was founded. Let any man living try to substantiate this calumny <note>A false and malicious statement designed to damage someone's reputation; slander.</note>, and it will be easier for him to prove that the Bank of England is bankrupt than to prove these claims.

<summary>The Theosophist clarifies that the Society has no endowment or capital and relies on meager voluntary donations, while strongly refuting rumors that Madame Blavatsky received large sums of money from wealthy members.</summary>

<header>THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.</header>

...amount that would suggest that the said “Founder” <note>Referring to Madame Helena Blavatsky.</note> has ever made any money out of Theosophy. These two calumnies <note>original: "calumnies"; false and defamatory statements; slanders.</note> were started by two high-born ladies belonging to the London aristocracy, and they have been immediately traced and disproved. They are the dead bodies—the carcasses of two fabrications—which, after having been buried in the sea of oblivion, are once more raised to the surface of the stagnant waters of slander.

**ENQUIRER.** Then I have been told of several large legacies <note>Property or money left to someone in a will.</note> left to the Theosophical Society. One—some £8,000—was left to it by an eccentric Englishman who did not even belong to the Society. The other—£3,000 or £4,000—was bequeathed <note>original: "testated"; left by means of a will.</note> by an Australian Fellow of the Theosophical Society <note>original: "F.T.S."</note>. Is this true?

**THEOSOPHIST.** I heard of the first; and I also know that, whether legally left or not, the Theosophical Society has never profited by it, nor have the Founders ever been officially notified of it. Because our Society was not then a chartered body <note>An officially incorporated organization with legal recognition.</note>, and thus had no legal existence, the Judge at the Court of Probate <note>The court responsible for handling the distribution of a deceased person's estate.</note>, as we were told, paid no attention to such a legacy and turned the sum over to the heirs. So much for the first case. As for the second, it is quite true. The testator <note>The person who wrote the will.</note> was one of our devoted Fellows and willed all he had to the Theosophical Society. But when the President, Colonel Olcott, came to look into the matter, he found that the testator had children whom he had disinherited for some family reasons. Therefore, he called a council, and it was decided that the legacy should be refused and the money passed to the legal heirs. The Theosophical Society would be untrue to its name were it to profit from money to which others are essentially <note>original: "virtually"</note> entitled—at any rate, based on Theosophical principles—if not legally.

**ENQUIRER.** Again—and I say this on the authority of your own journal, *The Theosophist*—there is a Rajah <note>original: "Rajah"; a title for a king or prince in India.</note> of India who donated 25,000 rupees to the Society. Have you not thanked him for his great generosity in the January 1888 issue of *The Theosophist*?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We did, in these words: “That the thanks of the Convention be conveyed to His Highness the Maharajah <note>original: "Maharajah"; a title for a "great king" or high-ranking prince in India.</note> . . . . for his promised munificent <note>original: "munificent"; extremely generous.</note> gift of 25,000 rupees to the Society’s Fund.” The thanks were properly delivered, but the money is still only a “promise” and has never reached the Headquarters.

**ENQUIRER.** But surely, if the Maharajah promised and received public thanks for his gift in print, he will keep his word?

**THEOSOPHIST.** He may, though the promise is eighteen months old. I am speaking of the present and not of the future.

**ENQUIRER.** Then how do you propose to continue?

**THEOSOPHIST.** As long as the Theosophical Society <note>original: "T.S."</note> has a few devoted members willing to work for it without reward or thanks, and as long as a few good Theosophists support it with occasional donations, it will exist, and nothing can crush it.

**ENQUIRER.** I have heard many Theosophists speak of a “power behind the Society” and of certain “Mahatmas” <note>Sanskrit for "Great Souls"; in Theosophy, these are believed to be highly evolved beings or spiritual masters who guide humanity.</note>—also mentioned in Mr. Sinnett’s <note>A.P. Sinnett (1840–1921), a prominent journalist and author whose books, like *Occult World*, introduced many to Theosophical concepts.</note> works—who are said to have founded the Society and who watch over and protect it.

**THEOSOPHIST.** You may laugh, but it is so.

If they can change lead into gold and create as much...

money as they like, besides performing all kinds of miracles at will, as related in Mr. Sinnett’s *The Occult World* <note>A.P. Because whatever they may or may not produce in the way of phenomena, they are not *counterfeiters* <note>original: "false coiners"</note>; nor would they place an additional and very strong temptation in the path of members and candidates: *Theosophy cannot be bought.* Until now, for the past fourteen years, not a single working member has ever received pay or a salary from either the Masters or the Society.

**ENQUIRER.** Then are none of your workers paid at all?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Until now, not one. But as everyone must eat, drink, and clothe themselves, all those who are without any financial means of their own and devote their whole time to the work of the Society are provided with the necessities of life at the Headquarters in Madras, India—though these “necessities” are humble enough, in truth! (See the Rules at the end of the book.) We hope to have a few members who will from now on be remunerated—if that word *can* be used in these specific cases. For every one of these Fellows <note>Full members of the Theosophical Society.</note> who are preparing to give *all* their time to the Society are leaving good professional positions with excellent prospects to work for us at *less than half* of their former salary.

**THEOSOPHIST.** Some of our members <note>original: "Fellows"</note> who are just a little wealthier than the rest provide support. The person who would speculate or try to make money from Theosophy would be unworthy to remain in our ranks.

**ENQUIRER.** But surely you must make money from your books, magazines, and other publications?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Among the magazines, only *The Theosophist* of Madras is profitable, and those profits have been regularly turned over to the Society year after year, as the published accounts show. *Lucifer* <note>A London-based magazine founded by Blavatsky; the title refers to the "Light-Bringer" (the morning star, Venus) rather than the Christian concept of the devil.</note> is slowly but steadily absorbing <note>original: "engulfing"</note> money, having never yet covered its own expenses—thanks to it being boycotted by religious <note>original: "pious"</note> booksellers and railway newsstands. *The Lotus* in France—which was started using the private and modest funds of a Theosophist who devoted all his time and labor to it—has ceased to exist for the same reasons, unfortunately! Nor is the New York *Path* self-sustaining <note>original: "pay its way"</note>, while the *Revue Théosophique* <note>original: French for "Theosophical Review"</note> of Paris has only just begun, also supported by the private funds of a female member. Furthermore, whenever any of the works published by the Theosophical Publishing Company in London do turn a profit, the proceeds will be devoted to the service of the Society.

**ENQUIRER.** And now please tell me all you can about the Mahatmas <note>Sanskrit for "Great Soul." In Theosophy, Mahatmas are believed to be highly evolved humans, or "Masters," who possess great wisdom and oversee the spiritual evolution of humanity.</note>. So many absurd and contradictory things are said about them that one does not know what to believe, and all sorts of ridiculous stories are circulating.

**THEOSOPHIST.** You are right to call them “ridiculous!”

<page-num>XIV.</page-num>

## THE "THEOSOPHICAL MAHATMAS"

---

### *ARE THEY "SPIRITS OF LIGHT" OR "GOBLINS DAMNED"?*
<note>original: "Goblins damn'd." This is a reference to William Shakespeare’s *Hamlet*, where Hamlet asks if the ghost is a "spirit of health or goblin damn’d."</note>

**ENQUIRER.** Who exactly are those you call your "Masters"? Some say they are "Spirits," or some other kind of supernatural beings, while others call them "myths."

**THEOSOPHIST.** They are neither. I once heard an outsider say to another that they were a sort of male *mermaids*, whatever such a creature might be. But if you listen to what people say, you will never have a true understanding <note>original: "conception"</note> of them. In the first place, they are *living men*, born as we are born, and destined <note>original: "doomed"</note> to die like *every* other mortal.

**ENQUIRER.** Yes, but it is rumored that some of them are a *thousand* years old. Is this true?

**THEOSOPHIST.** As true as the miraculous growth of hair on the head of Meredith’s *Shagpat* <note>A reference to George Meredith's 1856 fantasy novel, *The Shaving of Shagpat*, which draws on themes from the *Arabian Nights*.</note>. Truly, like the "Identical" <note>In Meredith's novel, the "Identical" is a single magical hair that makes its possessor powerful and must be shaved off.</note>, no *Theosophical* shaving has so far been able to *trim* <note>original: "crop"</note> it. The more we deny these claims, and the more we try to set people right, the more absurd these inventions become. I have heard of Methuselah <note>A biblical figure said to have lived 969 years.</note> being 969 years old; but, not being forced to *believe* in it,

<summary>This page addresses common misconceptions about the "Theosophical Mahatmas," clarifying that they are mortal, living men rather than spirits or mythical creatures. The author dismisses sensational rumors about their extreme longevity by comparing them to fictional literary characters.</summary>

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.

I had laughed at the statement, for which I was immediately <note>original: "forthwith"</note> regarded by many as a blasphemous heretic.

**ENQUIRER.** Seriously, though, do they outlive the normal lifespan of men?

**THEOSOPHIST.** What do you call a normal <note>original: "ordinary"</note> age? I remember reading in the *Lancet* <note>A world-renowned British medical journal established in 1823.</note> about a Mexican man who was almost 190 years old; but I have never heard of a mortal man—whether a regular person or an <term>Adept</term> <note>In Theosophy, an "Adept" is someone who has attained a high degree of spiritual knowledge and mastery over the laws of nature.</note>—who could live even half the years granted to Methuselah <note>A biblical figure said to have lived 969 years.</note>. Some Adepts do exceed, by quite a bit, what you would call a normal age; yet there is nothing miraculous about it, and very few of them care to live for a very long time.

**ENQUIRER.** But what does the word “Mahatma” really mean?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Simply a “great soul”—great because of moral elevation and intellectual achievement. If the title of "Great" is given to a drunken soldier like Alexander <note>Alexander the Great (356–323 BCE), the King of Macedon. The author contrasts military conquest with spiritual discovery.</note>, why should we not call those “Great” who have achieved far greater victories in uncovering Nature’s secrets than Alexander ever did on the battlefield? Besides, the term is an Indian <note>The word "Mahatma" comes from the Sanskrit words *maha* (great) and *atman* (soul).</note> and very ancient word.

**ENQUIRER.** And why do you call them “Masters”?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We call them “Masters” because they are our teachers, and because we have received all Theosophical truths from them—however poorly some of us may have expressed them, or others understood them. They are men of great learning, whom we call <term>Initiates</term> <note>Individuals who have been admitted into the inner or secret knowledge of a spiritual tradition or "mysteries."</note>, and they lead lives of even greater holiness. They are not ascetics <note>People who practice severe self-discipline and abstain from all forms of indulgence for spiritual reasons.</note> in the usual sense, though they certainly remain apart from the chaos <note>original: "turmoil"</note> and conflict of your Western world.

**ENQUIRER.** But is it not selfish for them to isolate themselves like this?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Where is the selfishness? Doesn't the fate of the Theoso-

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.

...and from them we derived whatever we can know. Does not the state of the Theosophical Society sufficiently prove that the world is neither ready to recognize them nor to profit by their teaching? Of what use would Professor Clerk Maxwell <note>James Clerk Maxwell (1831–1879), a famous Scottish physicist whose work laid the foundation for modern physics, including electromagnetism.</note> have been to instruct a class of little boys in their multiplication table? Besides, they isolate themselves only from the West. In their own country, they go about as publicly as other people do.

**ENQUIRER.** Don’t you attribute supernatural powers to them?

**THEOSOPHIST.** We believe in nothing supernatural, as I have told you already. Had Edison <note>Thomas Edison (1847–1931), the American inventor who developed the phonograph and the light bulb.</note> lived and invented his phonograph two hundred years ago, he would most probably have been burnt along with it, and the whole thing would have been attributed to the devil. The powers which they exercise are simply the development of hidden potentials <note>original: "potencies lying latent"</note> in every man and woman, the existence of which even official science is beginning to recognize.

**ENQUIRER.** Is it true that these men inspire some of your writers, and that many, if not all, of your Theosophical works were written under their dictation?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Some have been. There are passages entirely dictated by them word-for-word <note>original: "verbatim"</note>, but in most cases they only inspire the ideas and leave the literary form to the writers.

**ENQUIRER.** But this in itself is miraculous; it is, in fact, a miracle. How can they do it?

**THEOSOPHIST.** My dear Sir, you are laboring under a great mistake, and it is science itself that will disprove <note>original: "refute"</note> your arguments in the near future. Why should it be a “miracle,” as you call it? A miracle is supposed to mean some operation which is supernatural, whereas there is really nothing above or beyond NATURE and Nature’s laws. Among the many forms of the “miracle”...

<summary>This page argues that the "Masters" are not supernatural beings but individuals who have developed natural human potentials. It explains that their seclusion from the West is due to the world's unreadiness for their advanced teachings and describes how they inspire Theosophical literature.</summary>

<keywords>Theosophical Society, James Clerk Maxwell, Thomas Edison, phonograph, supernatural, latent potencies, inspiration, miracles, Nature's laws, Mahatmas</keywords>

<page-num>291</page-num>

# THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.

...under the misconception that these things are supernatural. Among the phenomena which have come under modern scientific recognition, there is Hypnotism, and one phase of its power is known as "Suggestion"—a form of thought transference which has been successfully used in combating particular physical diseases and other issues. The time is not far distant when the world of science will be forced to acknowledge that there exists as much interaction between one mind and another, regardless of the distance, as there is between one body and another in the closest contact. When two minds are sympathetically related, and the instruments through which they function <note>The "instruments" refer to the brain and the nervous system.</note> are tuned to respond magnetically and electrically to one another, there is nothing that will prevent the transmission of thoughts from one to the other at will. Since the mind is not of a physical <note>original: "tangible"</note> nature such that distance can divide it from the subject of its contemplation, it follows that the only difference that can exist between two minds is a difference of <term>STATE</term> <note>"State" here refers to a specific condition of consciousness or mental vibration.</note>. So, if this latter hindrance is overcome, where is the "miracle" of thought *transference*, regardless of the distance?

**ENQUIRER.** But you will admit that Hypnotism does nothing so miraculous or wonderful as that?

**THEOSOPHIST.** On the contrary, it is a well-established fact that a hypnotist can affect the brain of his subject so far as to produce an expression of his own thoughts, and even his words, through the body <note>original: "organism"</note> of his subject. Although the phenomena related to this method of actual thought transference are as yet few in number, no one, I presume, will attempt to say how far their action may extend in the future, once the laws that govern their production are more scientifically understood.

<summary>The author argues that thought transference is a natural scientific phenomenon similar to hypnotism and "suggestion," occurring when two minds are harmonized in the same state regardless of physical distance.</summary>
<keywords>hypnotism, suggestion, thought transference, mental interaction, science and the supernatural, state of consciousness</keywords>

**ENQUIRER.** Then why do our physicians not experiment and see if they could not do as much? <note>The author refers here to contemporary pioneers of hypnotic and psychological research listed in the footnote below.</note>

**THEOSOPHIST.** Because, first of all, they are not Adepts with a thorough understanding of the secrets and laws of the psychic and spiritual realms, but materialists, afraid to step outside the narrow groove of matter. Secondly, because they must fail at present, and indeed until they are brought to acknowledge that such powers are attainable.

**ENQUIRER.** And could they be taught?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Not unless they were first of all prepared by having the materialistic dross <note>Waste or impure matter; here referring to rigid materialistic beliefs.</note> they have accumulated in their brains swept away to the very last atom.

**ENQUIRER.** This is very interesting. Tell me, have the Adepts thus inspired or dictated to many of your Theosophists?

**THEOSOPHIST.** No, on the contrary, to very few. Such operations require special conditions. An unscrupulous but skilled Adept of the **Black Brotherhood** (whom we call “Brothers of the Shadow” and <term>Dugpas</term> <note>In Theosophical literature, "Dugpas" refers to practitioners of "black magic" or the "left-hand path," originally a term for a specific sect of Tibetan Buddhism but used here to denote spiritual antagonists.</note>) has far fewer difficulties to labor under. For—

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the ethics of psychic influence, contrasting the methods of the "Masters" with those of "black magicians" or "Dugpas."</meta>

<page-num>293</page-num>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

Because they have no spiritual laws to restrict their actions, such a <term>Dugpa</term> <note>original: "Dugpa"; in Theosophical terms, this refers to a practitioner of "black magic" or the "left-hand path," characterized by selfishness.</note> (a "sorcerer") will casually take control of any mind and subject it completely to his evil powers. But our Masters will never do that. They have no right—unless they wish to fall into Black Magic—to gain total control over anyone's immortal <term>Ego</term> <note>In Theosophy, the "Ego" refers to the permanent, reincarnating spiritual self, rather than the modern psychological definition.</note>. Therefore, they can only act upon the physical and <term>psychic</term> <note>Relating to the lower mind and emotions, which are still tied to the physical world.</note> nature of the person, leaving that person’s free will completely undisturbed.

Consequently, unless a person has entered into a psychic relationship with the Masters and is supported by their own full faith and devotion to their Teachers, the Masters face great difficulty when sending thoughts to them. When these conditions are not met, it is hard for them to penetrate the "cloudy chaos" of that person’s mental atmosphere. But this is not the place to discuss a subject of this nature. It is enough to say that if such a power exists, then there are conscious "Intelligences" (whether living in a body or not) who guide this power, as well as living, conscious instruments through whom it is transmitted and by whom it is received. We simply need to be careful of *black magic*.

**ENQUIRER.** But what do you actually mean by "black magic"?

**THEOSOPHIST.** Simply the abuse of psychic powers or any secret of nature; the act of applying the powers of <term>Occultism</term> <note>The study of hidden spiritual laws and the inner powers of the human mind.</note> toward selfish and sinful goals. A hypnotist who takes advantage of his powers of "suggestion" to force a subject to steal or murder would be called a black magician by us. The famous "rejuvenation system" of Dr. Brown-Séquard of Paris—which involves a disgusting injection of animal matter into human blood, a discovery all the medical journals of Europe are currently discussing—would be *unconscious black magic* if it were true. <note>Dr. Charles-Édouard Brown-Séquard (1817–1894) was a renowned physiologist who, in 1889, claimed to have discovered a "rejuvenating" elixir made from animal extract. It was a major controversy at the time this book was written.</note>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQUIRER.** But this is a medieval belief in witchcraft and sorcery! Even the Law itself has stopped believing in such things.

**THEOSOPHIST.** So much the worse for the law, as it has been led—through such a lack of discernment—into committing more than one judicial mistake and crime. It is only the word "sorcery" itself that frightens you because it sounds "superstitious." Wouldn’t the law punish an abuse of hypnotic powers, as I just mentioned? In fact, it has already punished such acts in France and Germany; yet it would indignantly deny that it was applying punishment to a crime of obvious sorcery. You cannot believe in the effectiveness and reality of the power of suggestion used by physicians and <term>mesmerists</term> <note>original: "mesmerisers"; practitioners of mesmerism, an early form of hypnotism named after Franz Mesmer.</note> (or hypnotists), and then refuse to believe in those same powers when they are used for evil motives. And if you do acknowledge that evil use, then you believe in Sorcery. You cannot believe in good while disbelieving in evil, or accept genuine money while refusing to believe in counterfeit coins. Nothing can exist without its contrast. No concept of day, light, or good could exist in your consciousness if there were no night, darkness, or evil to offset and contrast them.

**ENQUIRER.** Indeed, I have known men who—while thoroughly believing in what you call great psychic or magical powers—laughed at the very mention of Witchcraft and Sorcery.

**THEOSOPHIST.** What does that prove? Simply that they are illogical. Once again, so much the worse for them. Because we know of the existence of good and holy <term>Adepts</term> <note>A term for individuals who have achieved a high degree of mastery over their spiritual and physical nature.</note>, we believe just as firmly in the existence of bad and unholy Adepts—or **Dugpas**. <note>original: "Dugpas"; in this context, practitioners of black magic or those who use spiritual knowledge for selfish ends.</note>

**ENQUIRER.** But if the Masters exist, why don’t they appear publicly before everyone?
